#2144. Barcelona's Anthony Gordon 'stepped into Raphinha's shoes' against Sabah as Spanish media hail England international on historic night

By Matt Law | 26 Nov 2026 09:21, Last updated: 26 Nov 2026 09:29

Anthony Gordon scored twice in Barcelona's 4-1 win over Sabah in the league stage of the Champions League on Wednesday night.

In doing so, Gordon made history - the forward has now scored six Champions League goals in Azerbaijan, which is the most any English player has netted in away games in one country in the tournament.

Four of the England international's goals in Azerbaijan came against Qarabag during his time with Newcastle United, before scoring twice against Sabah on Wednesday.

Lamine Yamal missed out through injury, while Raphinha was rested from the start, with Gordon joined by Karim Adeyemi and Gabriel Jesus in the final third, and all three found the back of the net.

As expected, Gordon's performance brought a lot of praise from the Spanish media.

6 - Anthony Gordon's six UEFA Champions League goals in Azerbaijan are the most any English player has scored in away games in one country in the competition. Tourist. https://t.co/7OfdYkdzch

AS were extremely positive about Gordon's performance, saying that the forward was 'vital' to Barcelona securing all three points in the Champions League.

Mundo Deportivo also highlighted how well he combined with Gabriel Jesus and Adeyemi in the final third of the field in the absence of Yamal and Raphinha.

La Vanguardia concentrated on how Gordon became the main player in the Barcelona attack and said that he was one of the main reasons why Hansi Flick's side were able to score four goals.

Culemania said that Gordon 'stepped into Raphinha's shoes', having netted twice in the absence of the Brazil international, who has scored 19 times in all competitions this season.

Gordon will never be the main man in the Barcelona attack due to the presence of both Yamal and Raphinha, but he has played an important supporting role this term.

The 25-year-old's numbers for this season are relatively solid, coming up with five goals and five assists in 17 appearances in all competitions.

In La Liga, Gordon has two goals and five assists in 12 appearances, while he has struck three times in five outings in the Champions League.

The forward will be looking to improve his goal total in Spain's top flight, but it has been a positive start to his Barcelona career for the former Everton youngster.

#2143. Serie A, nuove regole per le maglie dei club dalla stagione 2027/28

Gli sponsor tecnici potranno collaborare con altri marchi; previste anche novità per stemmi, numerazioni e scadenze.

Cambiano le regole per le maglie dei club di Serie A. Il nuovo regolamento introdotto dalla Lega entrerà in vigore nella stagione 2027/28 e resterà valido fino al termine del campionato 2029/30. citando nss sports, le modifiche riguardano le collaborazioni tra marchi, gli stemmi, l’aspetto delle divise e i tempi di presentazione dei modelli.

CLICCA QUI E SEGUI LAZIOPRESS.IT ANCHE SU INSTAGRAM

La novità principale riguarda gli sponsor tecnici: potranno realizzare maglie da gioco in collaborazione con altri marchi, integrando il logo di questi ultimi nel proprio.

Le società potranno inoltre sostituire o integrare il proprio stemma con una versione celebrativa in occasione di anniversari o ricorrenze particolari. Per utilizzarla servirà la preventiva approvazione della Lega.

#2140. Leeds United news: Major Dominic Calvert-Lewin contract update ahead of January window?

By Carter White | 26 Nov 2026 09:38

Dominic Calvert-Lewin has reportedly signed a new Leeds United contract running until the summer of 2029.

Daniel Farke's side have continued their excellent start to the 2026-27 campaign following the November international break, recording a 4-1 success at the Stamford Bridge home of Chelsea on Saturday afternoon.

As a result of Lukas Nmecha and company collecting maximum points from the West London contest, the Elland Road club have stayed in seventh position in the Premier League standings, on the coattails of the Champions League conversation.

The main man in attack for Leeds in recent times, Calvert-Lewin has made a positive start to the season, with the 29-year-old Leeds star supposedly wanted by relegation-threatened Manchester United.

Nevertheless, the England international will be fully focused on helping Farke's troops qualify for European competition this term, with the Whites welcoming Coventry City to Yorkshire on Saturday.

According to The Athletic's Beren Cross via transfer expert Fabrizio Romano, Leeds United supporters have been given a major piece of positive news regarding the future of leading marksman Calvert-Lewin.

The report claims that the 29-year-old has agreed to an extension of his current terms at Elland Road, with the Three Lions appearance-maker originally joining on a free transfer from Everton in August 2025.

It is understood that Calvert-Lewin's fresh deal will run until the conclusion of the 2028-29 campaign, at which point the striker will be heading towards his mid-30s and the twilight of his career.

Before Leeds can officially announce the news surrounding the forward, it is believed that they must complete 'formal steps', with everyone involved in the process expecting a smooth process in that respect.

Either side of the November international break, Calvert-Lewin's side have recorded back-to-back wins over Tottenham Hotspur and Chelsea, proving their strength against some of the Premier League's biggest names.

Part of the furniture in the Premier League since the 2017-18 campaign, Calvert-Lewin has been the classic example of a confidence and purple-patch striker, with the attacker's form swinging dramatically.

This season, the 29-year-old is arguably churning out the best performances of his top-flight career to date, scoring five goals and providing one assist during the opening 11 Premier League games.

Whilst Leeds are known to be in the market for another striker ahead of the January window, there is no desire to replace Calvert-Lewin in the starting XI, as is clear with a contract extension on the table.

#2139. Brocchi: "L'Inter deve migliorare, ma scudetto non a rischio. Sucic, potenziale enorme"

Brocchi: "L'Inter deve migliorare, ma scudetto non a rischio. Sucic, potenziale enorme"

Scegli FcInter1908 come tuo sito preferito su Google: clicca qui

L'ex centrocampista Cristian Brocchi, nella sua analisi per sitiscommesse.com, ha parlato di Inter-Genoa: "A Bergamo l'Inter è stata tre volte avanti di due gol e si è fatta raggiungere sul 4-4: con 17 gol subiti ha la difesa peggiore fra le prime cinque della classifica. In quella partita ha ripetuto un errore già commesso in altre situazioni, quel calo di concentrazione che a volte hanno le squadre forti, che pensano magari di avere già la vittoria in tasca. Poi basta qualche piccolo sbaglio, una corsa in meno o una percezione del pericolo in meno per buttare via tutto quello che avevi costruito. Credo però che Chivu farà mea culpa e che in questi giorni abbia già analizzato gli errori e ci abbia lavorato. Non penso che per questi motivi lo scudetto sia a rischio, ma la squadra deve migliorare sotto questo aspetto, perché non può permettersi di continuare a commettere queste ingenuità, dovute più a cali di attenzione che a una mancanza di qualità.

Quella partita le è costata anche Calhanoglu, che si è fermato di nuovo, stavolta per un problema al flessore, e salterà la sfida con il Genoa: al suo posto è entrato Sucic, che poi ha segnato su rigore. Penso che Calhanoglu sia un giocatore fondamentale per l'Inter, ma è altrettanto vero che a centrocampo la squadra ha diversi giocatori in grado di fare bene e di mettere qualità, esperienza e voglia di vincere al servizio dei compagni. Sucic è uno di questi: ha un enorme potenziale e gli va data la fiducia giusta per rendere al 100%.

Dall'altra parte De Rossi ha raccontato che l'inizio di questa stagione è stato forse il momento più difficile della sua carriera e ha ringraziato il Genoa, che ha vinto quattro delle ultime cinque partite, per avergli dato «fiducia e pazienza» in un momento in cui altri club non ne hanno molta. Ha ragione, e ne sono felicissimo, perché è ancora una volta la dimostrazione che chi ha fiducia e pazienza può ottenere dei risultati. Oggi, dal mio punto di vista, il Genoa è la squadra gestita meglio: non si è fatto prendere dallo sconforto per tutte quelle sconfitte, non ha messo in discussione l'allenatore e gli ha sempre dato la fiducia necessaria per fare un ottimo lavoro. Adesso ne sta traendo beneficio, con punti e vittorie importanti. La pazienza che il Genoa ha avuto con De Rossi è purtroppo un'eccezione, in Italia ma ormai anche altrove, e deve servire da lezione alle tante società che non hanno mai la pazienza di lavorare con l'allenatore che hanno scelto anche nei momenti di difficoltà".

#2135. Liverpool: With Isak and Wirtz, the Reds’ transfer business takes off a year late

By Axel Clody | 26 Nov 2026 09:44

During the 2025 summer transfer window, Liverpool broke the bank, spending £410m.

Alexander Isak and Florian Wirtz were the two most expensive signings, as the Swedish striker arrived for £123m (€145m) and the German attacking midfielder for £106m (€125m). But for many months, the two most expensive recruits in the club's history failed to live up to expectations.

In his first season at Liverpool, Wirtz scored seven goals and provided 10 assists in 49 games, but his impact was largely disappointing. Isak, for his part, barely played (four goals and one assist in 22 games) because of a fractured fibula. That summer 2025 transfer window was thus described as 'the worst transfer window in Premier League history'.

Both attackers were therefore expected to deliver in their second season at Liverpool, with new manager Andoni Iraola arriving.

The momentum is completely different at the start of this campaign, as the 3-0 win over Club Brugge on Wednesday night in the Champions League illustrated perfectly. Isak scored twice, while Wirtz also found the net.

The Swede is rediscovering the efficiency that made his reputation at Newcastle United. He now has ten goals in all competitions in 16 games and has just scored in six consecutive away games for Liverpool. The 27-year-old is establishing himself as the number nine Liverpool had long been waiting for.

On Wednesday night, Isak was asked about when he would be back at his best level: 'It is always difficult to say, but I can definitely say that I feel good, the team is in a good rhythm, that is the most important thing' - he said.

After a season of adaptation to English football, in which he struggled to have an impact in the final third and to be decisive, Wirtz is in excellent form.

Still deployed as a number 10, the 23-year-old German has scored four goals and provided five assists in 17 games in all competitions for Liverpool.

His November has been excellent: three goals and two assists in three games for Liverpool, as well as three goals in two games for the German national team. Wirtz appears to have digested his huge transfer and to be ready to shed his 'flop' label at Liverpool.

#2133. Paris Saint-Germain vs. Lorient - prediction, team news, lineups

By Seye Omidiora | 26 Nov 2026 09:45, Last updated: 27 Nov 2026 10:21

Winless against Lorient in two years, Paris Saint-Germain welcome the 10th-placed visitors to Parc des Princes for Saturday's matchday 12 clash.

The defending Ligue 1 champions' 12-match winning streak was halted by Roma on Wednesday, but they now aim to extend their sequence of eight consecutive top-flight victories.

PSG had maintained a perfect record across all competitions for nearly three months before Roma visited in the Champions League in midweek, with the Serie A side battling to a 2-2 draw on matchday five.

That result ended the Parisians' four-match European winning streak, although Luis Enrique's men remain second in the league-phase standings and firmly inside the coveted top-eight places that guarantee last-16 qualification.

Returning to league action should please supporters, who have watched their side claim eight consecutive victories since losing to Monaco at the start of September, albeit with only three clean sheets.

However, eagle-eyed observers will note that the Red and Blues have won only one of their last five meetings with Lorient across the past four years, with three of the last four encounters ending level.

Another draw would please Paris FC, Lille and Lyon, who trail the capital club by three, five and six points respectively as the Ligue 1 title race begins to take shape.

Alexandre Dujeux therefore aims to prevent Lorient's first defeat in this fixture since April 2024; however, his own five-match winless record against PSG and four consecutive losses to Enrique are ominous signs for the 50-year-old manager.

An indifferent run of results further undermines confidence in Les Merlus extending their unbeaten sequence in this fixture, with the Brittany-based club winning only one of their last four league matches.

Admittedly, only one of those matches ended in defeat, a 2-0 home loss to Brest, while their solitary victory during that period came away at Le Havre.

However, that early-November success offers limited encouragement given that it came against the division's bottom side, who have collected a paltry two points from 11 matches.

Their overall away record is more encouraging, with Dujeux's team conceding just twice across five matches on the road, both in September's 2-1 defeat to Le Mans, while keeping clean sheets in the other four.

Another shutout would make Lorient the first team since Monaco in November last year to prevent PSG from scoring, a feat far easier said than done.

Paris Saint-Germain form (all competitions):

Khvicha Kvaratskhelia and Desire Doue are both sidelined with ACL injuries for PSG's fixture on Saturday.

Ousmane Dembele and Marquinhos are pushing for their first starts since last month's 4-2 victory over Barcelona, although Enrique may be tempted to retain the centre-back partnership of Ilya Zabarnyi and Willian Pacho.

Ferran Torres remains the capital club's leading Ligue 1 scorer with five goals, while Achraf Hakimi, Senny Mayulu and Marquinhos have three apiece.

Lorient have shared the goals around this season, with nine different players finding the net for the Brittany-based visitors.

Only Jean-Victor Makengo has scored more than once for Les Merlus, leaving Dujeux searching for someone to step up at Parc des Princes.

The visitors' prospects are further weakened by the absence of Panos Katseris, with a muscle injury ruling their leading goal provider out of this weekend's encounter.

Isaak Toure (groin) and Bandiougou Fadiga (muscle) are also sidelined for the 10th-placed side on matchday 12.

Paris Saint-Germain possible starting lineup:

Safonov; Hakimi, Pacho, Marquinhos, Mendes; Zaire-Emery, Vitinha, Ruiz; Akliouche, Dembele, Godts

Mvogo; Adjei, Talbi, Obaretin; Sylla, Cadiou, Ebong, Kouassi; Makengo, Bernardeau; Tosin

Lorient's four clean sheets from five away matches suggest that PSG may need patience, particularly against opponents who have repeatedly frustrated them in recent meetings.

However, the visitors' limited scoring threat and Katseris's absence should allow Enrique's men to sustain pressure and eventually secure a ninth consecutive league victory.

By Carter White | 26 Nov 2026 09:57

Leeds United have reportedly moved towards agreeing a contract extension for midfielder Ao Tanaka.

The Japan international has been a key part of Daniel Farke's side this season and started as the Whites smashed Chelsea by a 4-1 scoreline at Stamford Bridge in the Premier League last weekend.

Leading the line for the visitors in West London, Dominic-Calvert-Lewin is also supposedly on the verge of putting pen to paper on fresh terms at Elland Road, with Leeds closing in on the striker's renewed signature.

Losing just two matches in the top flight so far this term, Leeds are currently occupying seventh position in the Premier League standings, with the Yorkshire giants targeting European qualification.

Aiming to record three straight league wins for the first time this campaign, Farke's side will be the heavy favourites on Saturday afternoon, when they welcome former Championship foes Coventry City to Elland Road.

According to Sky Sports News, Leeds are moving decisively ahead of a busy festive period on the pitch, with the European hopefuls aiming to resolve the long-term futures of Calvert-Lewin and Tanaka.

The report states that the Whites are closing in on a contract extension for Japanese midfielder Tanaka, whose current deal at Elland Road expires at the conclusion of the 2027-28 domestic cycle.

It is understood that Farke's side have offered the ex-Fortuna Dusseldorf man an extra 12 months of contract security, meaning that he is set to be committed to the cause until June 2029.

At the beginning of the calendar year, there was a real question mark over whether Tanaka had a future in the first-team ranks of Leeds, who were comfortably avoiding a relegation battle without the 28-year-old in their starting XI.

However, the engine-room enforcer has since fought his way back to the front of the plans for the Whites, with the midfielder starting nine of the club's 11 Premier League fixtures in 2026-27 so far.

Given the positive trajectory of Leeds over the past few years, it is no surprise that contract extensions are the talk of the town at the moment, with the club's hierarchy keen to tie their best assets down.

On the pitch, Tanaka and Calvert-Lewin have been key to the first-team progression at Elland Road, with the Whites looking set to establish themselves as a Premier League regular over the coming years.

Bringing Leeds back up from the Championship in 2025, head coach Farke is also in line for a new contract, with the German tactician certainly deserving of such a reward in the near future.

#2129. Man United urged to sign £62.8m midfielder to fix "phenomenal" transfer mistakes (exclusive)

By Ben Knapton | 26 Nov 2026 10:16

Former Manchester United star Gordon Strachan - speaking exclusively to Sports Mole - has urged the Red Devils to sign a £62.8m midfielder to help correct their "phenomenal" transfer mistakes.

New Man Utd manager Mauricio Pochettino remains winless since replacing Michael Carrick following Wednesday's infuriating 2-2 draw with Sporting Lisbon in the Champions League, in which the Red Devils fell behind, took the lead and were then pegged back in Portugal.

The four-goal stalemate marked the seventh game in a row that Man United had conceded at least two goals, and once again, the Red Devils' open midfield was exposed.

The pairing of Youri Tielemans and Kobbie Mainoo did not cover themselves in glory against Liverpool or Sporting, and the former is now suspended for RB Leipzig in December after being sent off towards the end of Wednesday's game.

Andrey Santos has also not yet had the desired impact since joining from Chelsea, while fellow 22-year-old Carlos Baleba has played just 397 minutes across seven appearances due to injury problems.

Speaking to Sports Mole via William Hill betting site, Strachan - who scored 36 goals in 198 games for Man United - has told the Red Devils hierarchy to make a move for Crystal Palace lynchpin Adam Wharton to help offset the "phenomenal" amount of money the club has wasted on under-performing players.

"Adam Wharton is one of my picks," Strachan said. "Elliot Anderson was also another one they should have gone for. Tielemans has been doing well in the Premier League for nearly 10 years, so they're buying somebody who's at the latter stage of his career. I might be wrong, I hope I'm wrong, and hopefully, he’s got another five or six years in him.

"And I've got to say, the world of football in England is full of the top teams, especially Chelsea, Spurs, bringing in players who sign for £40m, £50m, you go, who is he? There's just so many players like this now.

"You see some of these players, and you go, what has he done to deserve a £45m fee? Baleba, Santos, you’re picking up people for big fees, who have no reputation.

"Who's been brave here? Is this just a kind of cosy midfield? You could go back to Mason Mount for £40m - who actually signed this off to pay this amount of money for players?

"It’s just beyond belief the money that Man United have wasted. It's phenomenal. And that's from the recruitment not doing their work properly.

"They've got to start saying, what is my plan for the next 10 years at Man United? And how do we go about this? Arsenal were a bit like that five or six years ago. It wasn't anywhere near as bad a situation as Man United, but they stuck to their plan, and it paid off."

Data from the 2026-27 season does not paint Wharton in the most positive light compared to his Man United peers, given Crystal Palace's atrocious start to the campaign.

Indeed, the England international only leads Baleba, Tielemans, Santos and Mainoo on games played, crosses per 90 and accurate crosses per 90, while also winning the fewest duels and registering the lowest percentage of successful passes.

However, Wharton has committed the fewest fouls per 90 minutes of the quintet, and he has been dispossessed on the second-fewest occasions per 90 minutes, only behind Santos.

Thanks to Crystal Palace's perilous position in the Premier League table - Thomas Frank's men sit bottom of the rankings with just seven points from nine games - interested parties could pick up Wharton for a discount if the Eagles are relegated to the Championship.

The 22-year-old is valued at €75m (£62.8m) by Transfermarkt and has three years left on his Palace contract, but Man United may have to fend off competition from Liverpool and Chelsea to land him in 2027.

Gordon Strachan was speaking to Sports Mole on behalf of William Hill Football Odds.

#2128. Brian Madjo, 17, electrifies Villa despite Super Cup defeat by PSG

Unai Emery’s Aston Villa 2.0 remain under construction but from the wreckage of a difficult summer there has emerged a potential star for the future.

Emery may have now suffered four successive defeats in the Super Cup, yet the big positive from a balmy night in Salzburg was the performance of teenage forward Brian Madjo. Aged 17 years and 212 days, Madjo became the youngest player to start and score in this continental showpiece, just over a week after he was given permission to play for his club.

Signed from Metz for £10m in January, Madjo had been prevented from playing because of Fifa’s rules on the international transfer of minors. Villa’s appeal to the Court of Arbitration for Sport to have him registered was upheld on August 4 and what a prospect he appears.

Born in Enfield but raised in Luxembourg, there has been a huge buzz around him since his arrival in the new year.

Paris St-Germain have produced an abundance of talented teenagers over the years and Madjo did not look out of place here.Désiré Doué’s second-half strike secured PSG the Super Cup – contested by the winners of the Champions League and Europa League – for the second successive year, but Emery will have seen enough to give him renewed confidence.

Desire Doue puts PSG 2-1 ahead despite originally being flagged offside 🫣📺 TNT Sports & HBO Max pic.twitter.com/vnMIZCWJaj

His team played with all the resilience and bravery that has become a trademark, epitomised by the powerful Madjo and another teenager in midfielder George Hemmings.

Emery said: “Brian has scored goals for us in pre-season and has shown his capacity to work in our idea. I am so happy because the young players were involved in our work and not playing last season.

“Of course there is still work to do but they showed they can play with us. They have responded fantastically and will then feel the confidence. With a loss you can never be happy but overall I am proud of all the players and everything we’ve done. Some changes will be needed but we’re confident.”

This was a fine Villa performance to silence the doom merchants who have been fearing a troubled season. After the painful sales of Morgan Rogers, Youri Tielemans and Lucas Digne, Villa are clearly a team in transition. They are operating under restrictions to comply with Uefa’s financial rules and there is still the possibility of another important player leaving before the transfer deadline day.

England centre-back Ezri Konsa remains a priority target for Premier League champions Arsenal, and was absent here. Konsa, Emiliano Martínez and Ollie Watkins were all granted extended leave after their participation in the World Cup, which has done little to ease the fears of supporters over more departures. The trio will return to training this weekend.

While some outsiders are predicting a difficult season for Villa, do not bother telling Emery. He has turned putting noses out of joint into an art form since sweeping into Villa Park and with him around anything feels possible.

The Basque remains optimistic about the future and those hopes will only have been underlined by the performance of Madjo. He bullied PSG centre-back Willian Pacho for much of the evening and even managed to steal the limelight from PSG’s magician Khvicha Kvaratskhelia.

Hemmings, 19, has emerged from the academy and already made a real impression on his manager. Despite the high regard for those youngsters, this always appeared likely to be a tough proposition against a full-strength PSG team. Indeed, before this game in Salzburg only 39 per cent of Europa League winners have beaten their Champions League rivals in this fixture.

The French champions did start slowly, but had been cranking up the pressure before taking a 20th minute lead with a spectacular strike from their Georgian superstar.

Doué forced a save from Marco Bizot and, after Villa failed to clear, the loose ball landed in the path of Kvaratskhelia, who cut inside on the left to his right foot before smashing a shot into the roof of the net.

Khvicha Kvaratskhelia puts PSG 1-0 ahead vs Aston Villa 🔥📺 TNT Sports & HBO Max pic.twitter.com/BIf8njpaSx

It was a strike of sheer power and accuracy, catching Bizot by surprise. PSG briefly appeared in control, but Villa responded superbly. In 17 minutes they created six goal-scoring chances before the outstanding Madjo finally made one of them count.

Before his goal, he had narrowly missed the target twice with a pair of headers from John McGinn’s crosses. Villa also had another opportunity 10 minutes before half-time, when Pacho’s mistake allowed Hemmings to run from the centre circle unopposed. As he entered the penalty area, the PSG defenders had raced back to recover and a promising moment ended with Madjo curling a shot wide.

Brian Madjo with a big chance to put Aston Villa level 😮📺 TNT Sports & HBO Max pic.twitter.com/pqsC5Hor15

PSG goalkeeper Matvey Safonov then produced his first save just before the break, turning away Boubacar Kamara’s close-range header. Villa did carve out another golden chance when Madjo’s strength took hm past Pacho, but he hit the outside of the post. Madjo’s moment did come, though, as he once again bullied Pacho to divert a McGinn cross into the roof of the net.

Brian Madjo at 17-years-old scores in the UEFA Super Cup Final 🤯📺 TNT Sports & HBO Max pic.twitter.com/PccX7FxjAr

Villa were buoyant, but it was PSG who scored the crucial second goal after Doué was awarded the goal following a VAR review. Doué was sent clear down the right by substitute Ousmane Dembélé and confidently beat Bizot, with reviews concluding that he had been played onside by Matty Cash.

The game was refereed by Omar Artan, the Somali referee who was supposed to officiate at the World Cup but was denied entry to the United States.

It became rather fractious as time began to tick away, with McGinn booked after complaining over the placement of a free-kick. Madjo was replaced by Tammy Abraham with 17 minutes left and this will be an evening he will never forget.

What will we learn from the “new Aston Villa” tonight? After the sales of Morgan Rogers, Youri Tielemans and Lucas Digne, this is a Villa team in transition.

Unai Emery virtually admitted last night it could be another slow start to the season, like last year. Villa face a full-strength PSG team hoping to win the Super Cup for the second time in a row.

I don’t think we can take much from this result tonight, whatever it is. Villa cannot be properly judged until the window closes. Don’t bother telling that to Unai Emery, though. He is ready to continue defying the doubters.

The Champions League and Europa League winners fight it out in the Uefa Super Cup in Salzburg as PSG take on Aston Villa. PSG took on English opposition in the Super Cup a year ago, when they played Tottenham. PSG fell 2-0 down in that game in Udine but scored two late goals to take it to penalties, where they won 4-3. These sides met in the Champions League knockout stages two seasons ago; PSG did progress from the quarter-final encounter as they went on to win the competition but not without a scare as Aston Villa did win at Villa Park and PSG only progressed 5-4 on aggregate. The two clubs have been involved in a transfer this week, with left-back Lucas Digne re-joining PSG from Aston Villa.

Aston Villa have played in the Super Cup once before back in 1982 when they took in Barcelona. They lost the first leg 1-0 over at Camp Nou but fought back to win the second leg 3-0 at Villa Park to claim the trophy. Aston Villa were last in action out in the Far East last Friday, falling to a 2-1 defeat to Bayern Munich in Hong Kong. It has been a big summer of change at the club, with key starters like Morgan Rogers and Youri Tielemans departing and the likes of Alejandro Garnacho and Joao Gomes arriving.

“I feel like the club is in a new era, with this possibility to fight for trophies,” Unai Emery told Uefa. “We are trying to be successful through, firstly, [building] a winning team, and then aiming for the positive consequences after it; winning trophies. To play the UEFA Super Cup is another opportunity to be there, the opportunity to win a trophy, to play against Paris Saint-Germain; the [best] team in the world now and another team we can compare [ourselves] with.”

Emery has confirmed that Ezri Konsa, Ollie Watkins and Emiliano Martinez will not be involved as they are still on their breaks following the World Cup.

PSG have now won back-to-back Champions Leagues, beating Arsenal in the final last season. They have only played a couple of friendlies in pre-season, losing 3-0 to Mallorca before drawing 1-1 against Manchester United. Luis Enrique’s side will take on Lens in the Trophée des Champions on Sunday before kicking off the defence of their Ligue 1 title at home to Rennes.

“I focus on renewing that desire to win again,” Enrique told Uefa. “It is difficult to keep winning. Right now, we have a unique challenge; something that is practically unprecedented in the history of European football, which is to draw level with Real Madrid if we can win a third consecutive Champions League. No-one in history has won three in a row, only Real Madrid. We can equal them. But I do not think there is any team in Europe more motivated than us.

“No-one rules out PSG any more. Two years ago, it was impossible to put us among the favourites; not impossible, but we were not one of the favourites. Now, no one would say that Paris Saint-Germain are not the favourites. That is something we have earned, but if we are going to maintain that position, we have to work even harder.”

PSG have added Monaco winger Maghnes Akliouche and Digne to their ranks this summer. They are also pushing to sign Ferran Torres from Barcelona and Mika Godts from Ajax. Bradley Barcola and Ibrahim Mbaye have both been linked with moves to Liverpool but have been included in their squad for tonight’s game, as have Akliouche and Digne.

#2127. Napoli, un big salta il Sassuolo: tanti rientri per Allegri ma scelte obbligate in mediana

Il Napoli non può sbagliare. La squadra di Max Allegri domani sarà di scena sul campo del Sassuolo con l'obiettivo di dare continuità alla grande prova di Manchester, ma non solo. Il Napoli deve ancora centrare due vittorie di fila in campionato e quindi, dopo il successo col Torino, ha una grande occasione (Allegri invece non vince due gare di fila addirittura da marzo, inserendo anche il percorso al Milan). Di fronte ci sarà un Sassuolo in crisi, reduce da 6 sconfitte consecutive ed in generale ha già perso 8 delle prime 12 gare giocate. Il Napoli però non può considerarsi del tutto fuori dal tunnel e non ha margine d'errore se vuole risalire la classifica, iniziando da subito ad approfittare ad esempio dello scontro diretto tra Como e Juventus. Allegri intanto sarà costretto di nuovo a cambiare qualcosa.

Nessun rischio per Kevin De Bruyne che dovrebbe saltare la gara col Sassuolo. Il belga ha svolto differenziato per un leggero affaticamento muscolare: nulla di grave, ma quanto basta per restare fuori evitando che il fastidio possa diventare qualcosa di più serio. L'ultima cosa di cui il Napoli ha bisogno. Nonostante i rientri, a centrocampo Allegri è rimaneggiato con i soli Anguissa, Gilmour e McTominay è per questo non è escluso che faccia a meno del 4-3-3 per magari tenersi anche un cambio in panchina.

L'emergenza delle ultime gare, con un'intera squadra fuori, può considerarsi però alle spalle. Rispetto alla Champions infatti Allegri potrà contare nuovamente su Rafa Marin che era squalificato, su Marianucci e Favasuli che sono fuori lista in Europa mentre David Neres (che era in panchina col City) dopo ulteriori giorni di lavoro può essere all'occorrenza un'arma importante per il finale di partita. Col Lecce, dopo una settimana tipo di lavoro, dovrebbero poi esserci i recuperi anche di Di Lorenzo e Badiashile.

#2126. Bournemouth vs. Celta Vigo: How the Cherries could equal Chelsea, Leeds feat with Europa League victory

By Carter White | 26 Nov 2026 10:27

Bournemouth have prepared for their Europa League clash at Celta Vigo in the knowledge that they could smash even more history.

Marco Rose's side won just two of their opening eight competitive matches of the 2026-27 season, hinting that there could be a period of struggle following Andoni Iraola's departure to Liverpool.

However, with the Cherries set for a Celta Vigo challenge on Thursday night, it is clear that the Dean Court club are thriving, with the visitors to Spain sitting in fifth position in the Premier League standings.

A star of the current positive form, Evanilson has truly announced himself as an elite top-flight striker, netting seven goals and providing two assists in his past eight appearances, scoring in five consecutive league fixtures.

What makes Bournemouth's run even more impressive is that last season's star man Junior Kroupi has barely started a competitive match so far after recovering from a broken leg sustained over the summer.

Starting with a 3-0 Europa League success over Austrian side Sturm Graz on October 15, Bournemouth have recorded nine straight victories in all competitions across the Premier League, EFL Cup and European action.

That winning streak is the longest since the Cherries became a Football League club at the beginning of the 1923-24 season, showcasing how impressively the South Coast team are performing at the moment.

However, there are still more records to be achieved over the coming weeks, with Bournemouth aiming to become only the eighth team to record 10 straight competitive victories whilst playing in the Premier League.

Winning the Premier League during the 2016-17 campaign under Antonio Conte, Chelsea managed 10 wins in a row in all competitions, with that particular streak running from October 2016 until New Year's Eve 2016.

Leeds are the other club to post that exact number of wins in succession, with the Elland Road side completing the feat between August and October during the 1999-00 Premier League term.

The dominant force in English domestic football during the late 2010s and early 2020s, Manchester City are unsurprisingly the club with the most consecutive competitive wins as a Premier League side.

Under the expert stewardship of Pep Guardiola, the title-winning Citizens won 21 straight matches between December 2020 and March 2021, destroying Arsenal's previous record of 13 games in succession.

To match Man City's record, Bournemouth would need to secure victory in Spain on Thursday before winning every single match up to and including the trip to Brighton & Hove Albion on January 5.

#2124. Luton Town vs Oxford United - prediction, team news, lineups

By Calum Burrowes | 26 Nov 2026 10:55, Last updated: 26 Nov 2026 20:05

Two former Arsenal teammates come head to head on Saturday in League One as in-form Luton Town welcome high-flying Oxford United to Kenilworth Road.

Jack Wilshere's side will be looking to extend their unbeaten run to nine matches in all competitions after coming from behind to win 2-1 last weekend, while Aaron Ramsey and his U's will be aiming to move into the automatic promotion places by securing a fourth win in five third-tier outings.

More than six months after Luton Town narrowly missed out on a place in the League One playoffs last season, finishing just one point behind sixth-placed Leyton Orient, the Hatters have made a positive start to the 2026-27 campaign as they look to go one step better this time around.

Wilshere's side began the new season inconsistently and were sitting 16th after seven third-tier matches at the end of September, but have since enjoyed an impressive upturn in form, losing just once in the league across October and November, a 2-1 defeat to MK Dons at Stadium MK.

That run has seen Luton win six of their last eight league matches, while also progressing to the second round of the FA Cup and the knockout stages of the EFL Trophy, putting them firmly back in the playoff picture.

In fact, the Hatters could move into second place heading into the final month of the calendar year if they secure a ninth league victory of the season and other results go their way this weekend, with their recent form having lifted them more than 10 places into fifth and guaranteed them a place in the top six until at least their 17th league match next week, when they face struggling Blackpool.

A key factor behind their turnaround has been an improvement in front of goal, with Luton scoring 17 times in League One since the start of October, an average of more than two per game that has taken their overall tally to 29, a record bettered by just one side in the division.

As for the visitors, Oxford United make the 66-mile journey to Bedfordshire in similarly strong form and will be looking to move into December in the top two by recording a third consecutive victory.

The U's, led by former Wales midfielder Ramsey, have enjoyed a more consistent campaign than their upcoming opponents, having already moved into the top six by their seventh match of the 2026-27 season, when Luton were sitting 16th, and have continued to build on that strong start in recent weeks to climb to third.

Oxford head into their 15th league outing of the season with 29 points following eight wins, five draws and just one defeat, and they could move level on points with leaders Stockport County this weekend while still having two games in hand on Jimmy McNulty's side.

Although Oxford's FA Cup campaign came to an end at the first hurdle with a 1-0 home defeat to Stevenage, their form in the third tier has been exceptional, with no side in the top four divisions of English football losing fewer league matches than the U's.

Only Ramsey and Wilshere's former club Arsenal and League Two side Barnet have matched their tally of just one defeat.

While Luton have built their campaign around a clinical attacking unit, Oxford's promotion push has been underpinned by a strong defensive record, with only three sides in League One having conceded fewer than their 14 goals.

However, shipping three at home to Wycombe Wanderers last weekend, despite coming from three goals behind to secure a remarkable 4-3 victory, represented a rare blemish on an otherwise impressive defensive campaign.

The pair meet for the first time in just under two years on Saturday and will lock horns in a third-tier fixture for the first time since 2019, with Luton having enjoyed a spell in the Premier League since their previous meeting at this level.

Luton Town will be forced into at least one change following their weekend victory over Burton Albion after Jordan Clark was forced off with an injury in the 33rd minute, with the midfielder expected to miss out and potentially be replaced by Daniel Gore.

Clark is set to join fellow injury absentees Hakeem Odoffin, Gideon Kodua and Shayden Morris in the treatment room, with the latter yet to feature at all this season.

Former Bristol City forward Nahki Wells marked his 15th league appearance of the campaign with his eighth and ninth goals of the season, scoring a brace against the Brewers last Saturday, and the Bermudian is expected to lead the line once again.

Having been in EFL Trophy action during the week, beating Tottenham Hotspur Under-21s 2-1, Ramsey is expected to make wholesale changes for Oxford's return to League One action.

Jamie Cumming started in goal for the midweek cup tie and could retain his place ahead of Simon Eastwood, who conceded three times last weekend, while changes are also expected throughout the defence, midfield and attack.

Gatlin O'Donkor came off the bench to score a dramatic winner five minutes into stoppage time against Wycombe and could therefore be rewarded with a starting place on Saturday, potentially at the expense of Tyler Goodrham.

Shea; Jones, Mengi, Andersen, Johnston; Walsh; Richards, Donley, Gore, Palmer; Wells

Cumming; Kioso, Long, Brown, Currie; Brannagan, Andrews; Missanga, O'Donkor, Emakhu; Harris

While Oxford United arrive at Kenilworth Road in high spirits, they face a side who have collected more points than any other team in the division across their last six league games, meaning the U's will know they face a difficult task to win at a ground where they have not tasted victory since 2017.

With that said, Oxford did manage to come away from their last visit to Luton with a point, albeit more than two years ago, and we expect them to repeat that feat, with the two in form sides playing out a 2-2 draw.

#2123. Endrick e Bruno Fernandes al Milan: arrivano due risposte secche. Ma Cardinale...

Dall'estero, stanno provando a mettere i bastoni fra le ruote al calciomercato del Milan. Non che non sia permesso eh, anzi: è tutto lecito, giusto, probabilmente anche doveroso, mettendo i panni dei club proprietari del cartellino. Ma i rossoneri non si arrenderanno così facilmente. Ci sono due grandi obiettivi per Gerry Cardinale nei prossimi due mesi: Endrick del Real Madrid e Bruno Fernandes del Manchester United.

José Mourinho, tecnico dei Blancos, è intervenuto ieri in conferenza stampa parlando brevemente annche del prossimo mercato di gennaio, nel quale non vuole perdere nemmeno un giocatore dell'attuale rosa: "Non c’è nessun giocatore che non mi piaccia o che non voglia qui. Non voglio nessuno sul mercato, lo dico già da ora che mancano ancora due mesi al caos. Non voglio che se ne vada nessuno, le porte sono chiuse. Non voglio parlare di mercato e non voglio sentire notizie di mercato perché non le voglio". Una chiusura alla cessione di Endrick, pare, ma il Milan non mollerà.

Il Milan attenderà gli sviluppi per capire se regalare il giocatore ad Amorim la prossima estate.

#2122. Selfie-seekers bring Man Utd vs Leeds match to premature end

Aren’t friendlies supposed to be non-events? Manchester United against Leeds United in Dublin always had the capacity to be lively, but try explaining the half hour that Bruno Fernandes experienced in one breath. It would be impossible.

There was only one player the vast majority of the crowd wanted to see in the grandiosely named Boyle Sports Cup and, in chronological order from his 60th minute introduction, he received a standing ovation, hit the bar, had a goal disallowed, missed a penalty in a shoot-out and lifted a trophy.

Most remarkably – and, certainly, worryingly – of all, he was central to the game being ended early as five young boys, one after the other, breached security and ran to him in the middle of pitch, all of them successfully posing for photos with the Portuguese.

You could say it was harmless fun but it should not have happened and the flummoxed faces that were pulled on the Manchester United bench, as an army in high-visibility jackets stood and watched it all unfold, suggested they took a dim view.

It was a shame because in that cameo, Fernandes showed he has returned from the World Cup in excellent shape, the tempo of United’s play immediately lifting when he had the ball. It is wrong to make conclusions in the summer, but you are on safe ground to state he will be integral to their ambitions.

Croke Park is a stadium steeped in history and another chapter was penned here, as the capacity crowd made this the highest attended soccer match in Ireland with 82,000 coming through the gates: it is a magnificent venue, one that would be well capable of hosting a Champions League final.

Michael Carrick hopes in years to come that is the stage where his team will be but, right now, it is a case of learning to walk again before they can run and he more than anyone will know how far off that target is, not that it will stop him trying to get there.

The one thing that is obvious about the way United play under him is the first thought from everyone is to go forward quickly. Yes, this was only a friendly, but the ambition to be on the front foot and cause problems was evident from the start.

The team in red had much the better of the opening exchanges, with Diogo Dalot having the first shot that went over but, unlike on July 25, when Mayo beat Kerry here in Gaelic football’s All Ireland final, which went viral, nobody was cheering the sight of a ball clearing the bar.

It was like a home game for United and the majority got what they wanted in the 15th minute when a slick counter-attack ended with Bryan Mbuemo sliding a pass into Joshua Zikzee, who rifled past James Trafford with a first-time finish.

Devastating counterattack! 💥Manchester United break and Joshua Zirkzee is there to finish it off 🤩 pic.twitter.com/y6QHbZMi7n

Zirkzee is unlikely to be part of Carrick’s squad beyond the transfer window, with interest from clubs in Italy, and it would be disingenuous to suggest his career at Old Trafford has been successful, but his attitude was exemplary and he played as if he was desperate to change his manager’s mind.

There may have been an anticipation that United would stride clear, but this game was also about Leeds, whose summer has been encouraging and whose confidence from staying up last season has evidently enabled them to blossom.

You would be way off the mark if you include them in your conversations about which clubs are going to be relegated next May, and the sense they showed to stay with Daniel Farke has paid off. They have bought some good players, not least goalkeeper James Trafford and Harry Wilson.

Top stop from James Trafford! 🚫The Leeds United keeper has been busy in the first half... pic.twitter.com/VKmxNVKBsA

Trafford has done the right thing getting away from Manchester City and you could argue that he is already reaping the benefit of playing for a manager who wants him, as he made a fabulous save in the 23rd minute to thwart Mbuemo. It was all part of performance that led to him being named player of the match.

He was involved in the move that brought the Leeds equaliser, too, as his long clearance caused uncertainty and led to Tyler Fletcher being pickpocketed by Dan James and he nudged a pass into Brenden Aaronson, who kept his cool and danced into the area, ignoring Senne Lammens, to finish.

Leeds hit back with their first shot on target! 💥Brenden Aaronson with a cool finish 🥶 pic.twitter.com/5WsSZY41j6

It was an entertaining affair, mercifully lacking the bile that would usually bubble away when it is staged in England, and the longer the game advanced, the better Leeds looked. They finished the opening period strongly and came back after the restart, with 10 changes, in the same fettle.

Farke, whose team had shown their ability to score heavily when they pummelled Liverpool 4-2 in Chicago 10 days ago, would have taken plenty of encouragement, but the perfectionist in him would have cursed the opportunities that were squandered early in the second period.

The misses proved costly as the game meandered and the shoot-out went United’s way, after Wilson and James Justin failed from 12 yards, with Noussair Mazraoui thumping in the decisive kick. It really is never dull.

Good evening and welcome to live coverage of the pre-season friendly between Manchester United and Leeds United at a sold-out Croke Park in Dublin. Last year Leeds played Milan in the Irish capital and drew a crowd of 52,000 but at the GAA citadel, as I think I’m contractually obliged to call it, there will be 83,000 present, a testament to the Premier League’s reach and these two clubs’ popularity in Ireland founded on the exploits of Billy Whelan, Noel Cantwell, Johnny Giles, Roy Keane, Gary Kelly et al. It’s the first club match ever held at the stadium.

It’s a fixture that always stirs the blood and whenever it comes around you can guarantee that there will be reams of stuff about the War of the Roses, clash in styles, Busby and Revie, Bobby and Jack, large swathes of which this author has been guilty of producing. I’ll spare you all that today because while it’s relevant to fans of both clubs, they know it backwards. And it’s a match that has come around a wee bit too frequently in pre-season recently, first in Perth, Western Australia, in 2019 when Ole Gunnar Solskjaer’s side beat Marcelo Bielsa’s 4-0, then in 2023 in Oslo when Erik ten Haag’s second or third string defeated Daniel Farke’s threadbare, just relegated squad 2-0 and last year in Stockholm, which ended in a 0-0 draw between two scratch sides.

This one comes round later in the pre-season schedule than those three so we can expect to see stronger XIs, at least for part of the game. Leeds’ pre-season has gone well: after a 3-2 defeat by Wrexham in Tampa, they have beaten Sunderland in New Jersey, Liverpool in Chicago and RB Leipzig at Elland Road. Manchester United also lost to Wrexham (in Helsinki) then beat Rosenborg in Trondheim, Atlético in Stockholm and drew one apiece with the European champions, PSG, in Gothenburg last time out.

While Leeds will have new signing James Trafford available and Noah Okafor, who scored twice in Leeds’ first league victory at Old Trafford for 45 years last April, in the squad for the first time after the World Cup, Manchester United have Kobbie Mainoo and Marcus Rashford back in training since the start of the week but it seems too soon for them to start but Youri Tielemans is pushing to make his first start.

#2121. Nice vs. Troyes - prediction, team news, lineups

By Seye Omidiora | 26 Nov 2026 10:58

Nice return to Ligue 1 action a week after Paris Saint-Germain ended their three-match winning streak, with promoted Troyes visiting Allianz Riviera on matchday 12.

The Europe-chasing hosts remain one of the division's form teams and will back themselves to overcome a promoted side they have beaten in their last three meetings.

Olivier Pantaloni and his players faced a formidable challenge against PSG last weekend, with the defending Ligue 1 champions arriving on a seven-match winning streak.

While PSG extended their sequence of top-flight victories to eight, Nice's three-match winning run ended against the Parisians, hardly a disgrace given their opponents' quality and form.

Les Aiglons remain one of Ligue 1's form teams, with their 12 points from the last available 18 surpassed by only three clubs: PSG (18), Marseille (15) and Brest (13).

Back at home just days after PSG ended their eight-month unbeaten run on their own turf, Pantaloni's men will target maximum points to reclaim a European place, with sixth-placed Brest and fifth-placed Marseille sitting one and two points above Nice respectively.

That ambition appears reasonable given the Eaglets' three consecutive victories in this fixture and four wins in the last five meetings, with the visitors needing a strong performance to beat Nice at Allianz Riviera for the first time since 2017.

Stephane Dumont's men will fancy their chances of a positive result as they enter matchday 12 having collected six points from the last available nine.

Their only defeat during that period was an understandable 1-0 reverse at PSG, a performance that offered ESTAC encouragement in their bid to avoid an immediate return to Ligue 2.

Troyes' recent improvement follows a six-match winless run that included five consecutive defeats, with last season's Ligue 2 champions failing to score in four of those losses.

Collecting six points from their recent fixtures has lifted them out of the relegation zone and into 14th, although Dumont's men sit just one point above Strasbourg in the relegation playoff spot and three clear of second-bottom Angers.

While a disastrous weekend could leave the top-flight returnees in the bottom three, that would require victories for Auxerre, Strasbourg and Angers, with the latter also needing a six-goal swing.

Mohamed Abdelmonem (hamstring) and Elye Wahi are doubtful, although the six-goal forward is expected to feature for the hosts this weekend.

Laurent Abergel and Antoine Mendy remain unavailable due to long-term knee injuries, while Moise Bombito is also sidelined with a leg problem.

Beyond Wahi, Jonathan Clauss (three goals, three assists), Mohamed Amoura (three goals, two assists) and Sofiane Diop (two goals, three assists) all possess match-winning quality for the Eaglets.

Troyes' leading attacking threats are last weekend's two-goal hero Antoine Mille and Renaud Ripart, who remain the visitors' only players to have scored more than once in 2026-27.

Merwan Ifnaoui faces a pre-match assessment to determine his availability this weekend, while Paolo Gozzi (hamstring) and Yacouba Kone (knee) are sidelined.

Diouf; Seidu, Ndayishimiye, Mandza; Clauss, Witsel, Boudaoui, Nkounkou; Diop, Amoura; Wahi

La qualificazione è arrivata nel doppio decisivo, vinto da Lorenzo Musetti e Andrea Vavassori al tie-break del terzo set.

#2118. Como, Nico Paz non è poi così convinto di un ritorno al Real. Il Manchester United lo attira

Sono i numeri a spiegare perché il nome di Nico Paz è sulla bocca di tutti. Il classe 2004 ha trovato al Como la continuità e la maturità che cercava, fino alla consacrazione in Champions League, e con la maglia biancoblù ha messo a segno sei reti in stagione, tre delle quali in Europa. Tra queste spicca la doppietta al Manchester United, che ha fatto drizzare le antenne ai piani alti. Idolo dei tifosi lariani, con l'Argentina di Lionel Scaloni ha incantato a suon di danze sul pallone e di mancino fatato, tanto da essere considerato tra i principali candidati a ereditare la 10 di Leo Messi dopo il suo addio.

La questione più delicata riguarda il Real Madrid. Dopo l'enorme crescita di valore del giocatore, la scorsa estate i Blancos hanno rivisto gli accordi sul riscatto: il Como ha pagato 60 milioni di euro per tenerlo almeno un'altra stagione, mentre la clausola di riacquisto è stata fissata a 80 milioni e può essere esercitata solo nell'estate del 2027. In estate il club spagnolo potrebbe quindi bussare di nuovo alla porta, ma stando a quanto rivelato da The Athletic il ritorno dai blancos non è cosa decisa. Il trequartista argentino, infatti, senza garanzie di un posto da titolare non darebbe il suo consenso al trasferimento.

In questo scenario si fa strada il Manchester United, che ha inserito Nico Paz nella lista di obiettivi nel caso in cui Bruno Fernandes lasci Old Trafford. Il capitano dei Red Devils ha infatti fermato le trattative per il rinnovo del contratto, in scadenza a fine stagione, ma il club ha risposto attivando l'opzione per un ulteriore anno, così da assicurarsi un indennizzo se il portoghese partirà tra gennaio e giugno. Oggi c'è Mauricio Pochettino in panchina, dopo l'ennesimo terremoto interno, ma lo United conserva il suo appeal e attira la stella argentina: non sarà il club più desiderato dall'argentino, ma il gradimento per la destinazione ci sarebbe. La qualificazione alla Champions League sarà una condizione imprescindibile però. Combinazione? Il 22enne condivide gli stessi agenti di Pochettino, che peraltro non ha mai nascosto la propria stima per il giocatore.

By Carter White | 26 Nov 2026 11:09

Premier League clubs are reportedly interested in signing Fenerbahce defender Archie Brown during the January transfer window.

The 24-year-old has followed in the footsteps of Jude Bellingham and Jobe Bellingham in recent years, leaving the English domestic scene to forge a successful football career on the continent.

Brown has been a regular appearance-maker for the Turkish giants this season and played the full 90 minutes on Wednesday night, when the Turkish Super Lig club beat Shakhtar Donetsk in the Champions League.

With his performances catching the eye in Europe this term, Fenerbahce man Brown is supposedly admired by a number of high-profile clubs, including Nottingham Forest of the Premier League.

The highlight of the youngster's season so far undoubtedly arrived on November 4 when he found the net and provided an assist during the Yellow Canaries' 3-2 Champions League success over Liverpool.

According to German transfer specialist Florian Plettenberg, Fenerbahce defender Brown is attracting interest from across the continent following an impressive opening to the 2026-27 season.

The report claims that a number of Premier League clubs are keen on bringing the 24-year-old back to English shores, with the winter transfer window opening at the turn of the calendar year on January 1.

Providing encouragement for those sides eyeing up a swoop for Brown, the left-back supposedly has a release clause totalling £19.2m in his current contract with the Super Lig powerhouses.

It is understood that the stipulation can be triggered either during the upcoming January or summer transfer windows, meaning that potential suitors have options in their hunt for the player.

Since a 2-1 home defeat by Goztepe on October 31, Brown and Fenerbahce have strung together a four-game unbeaten run (W3 D1), a streak which includes that historic home success over Liverpool.

Brown spent a large chunk of his academy time within the ranks of East Midlands club Derby County, making 27 appearances for their Under-21s side before commencing his professional career abroad.

The 23-year-old started at Swiss outfit Lausanne in the Swiss Super League following a switch in June 2021, playing 43 matches and scoring five goals across a two-year stint at Stade de la Tuiliere.

The former Rams youngsters truly made a name for himself at Belgium side Gent before securing a high-profile transfer to Turkish and European giants Fenerbahce during the summer of 2025.

#2115. Il peso dell’assenza di Taylor

L’olandese potrebbe mancare anche contro l’Atalanta; Pedraza sarà confermato, mentre Leite è favorito su Doekhi.

Kenneth Taylor sarà assente contro il Torino e potrebbe saltare anche la gara con l’Atalanta. Secondo quanto riportato da Il Messaggero, Il problema muscolare lo terrà fuori per una o due settimane, con la speranza che lo stop non si prolunghi. Per sostituirlo, Gattuso ha scelto di partire con Dele-Bashiru, mantenendo l’assetto della Lazio.

CLICCA QUI E SEGUI LAZIOPRESS.IT ANCHE SU INSTAGRAM

Il centrocampista olandese si aggiunge agli altri giocatori colpiti da problemi muscolari: Rovella è recuperato, Zaccagni è pronto almeno per la panchina, mentre nell’elenco figurano anche Marusic, Sutalo e Tavares. L’assenza di Taylor priva il tecnico di un giocatore con caratteristiche difficili da ritrovare nella rosa.

L’ex Ajax partecipa alla costruzione del gioco, segna e crea occasioni per i compagni. Contro il Lecce, un suo colpo di tacco ha mandato in gol Noslin. Alle qualità offensive unisce una capacità di difendere che lo distingue da altri giocatori di talento.

Nelle prime dodici giornate, Taylor ha preso parte a cinque gol della Lazio: ha segnato contro Parma e Sassuolo, servito assist contro Sassuolo e Lecce e conquistato il rigore contro il Venezia. Dopo l’arrivo a Formello a gennaio, nella gestione Sarri, aveva raccolto tre reti in 17 presenze — una contro il Genoa e due contro il Bologna — e due assist, contro Parma e Napoli. La sua crescita è stata facilitata anche dall’arrivo di Frattesi.

Alla tredicesima giornata la Lazio si presenta a Torino da prima in classifica. Giocando prima delle rivali, può mettere pressione soprattutto a Roma e Inter: una vittoria sarebbe la sesta lontano dall’Olimpico, mentre un pareggio porterebbe a sette le trasferte consecutive senza sconfitte.

Gattuso aveva due possibilità: affidarsi a Dele-Bashiru, più potente ma meno tattico di Taylor, oppure passare al 4-2-3-1, provato più volte in allenamento da luglio. Per ora ha preferito la prima soluzione, senza interrompere la sequenza avviata alla prima di campionato al Dall’Ara e proseguita fino al 3-0 contro il Lecce.

Il tecnico avrebbe potuto puntare anche su Gudmundsson per la sfida con il Torino. La fisicità dei granata lo ha però orientato verso una scelta graduale: Dele-Bashiru partirà dall’inizio, con la possibilità di intervenire a gara in corso secondo l’andamento della partita.

Il nigeriano somiglia più a Frattesi che a Taylor. Gattuso gli chiederà quindi una maggiore attenzione difensiva rispetto a quella che offre abitualmente. Cambierà un interprete, non la disposizione tattica né la mentalità della Lazio.

#2114. FC Cincinnati vs Nashville SC - prediction, team news, lineups

By Darren Plant | 26 Nov 2026 11:30

FC Cincinnati play host to Nashville SC on Saturday night looking to book their place in the conference semi-finals of the MLS Cup playoffs.

The home side hold a 1-0 advantage in the best-of-three tie having earned a surprise victory on away soil in the opening fixture.

Having booked their place in the MLS Cup playoffs as far back as September 19, the assumption was that Nashville SC would have too much quality for FC Cincinnati, who finished the regular Eastern Conference campaign with 31 fewer points.

Nashville had also defeated Cincinnati in the final game of the regular season by a 2-0 scoreline on away territory.

Nevertheless, an early own goal from Nashville midfielder Patrick Yazbek gave Cincinnati a 1-0 victory at Geodis Park on November 22.

That marked Nashville's first defeat since September 10, ending a 10-match unbeaten streak that featured seven wins and three draws.

B. J. Callaghan will have concerns that their efforts throughout 2026 are close to going to waste, with Nashville having failed to score in two of their last four matches.

However, Nashville make the trip to the TQL Stadium on a five-game unbeaten run on their travels, while just 11 goals were conceded in 17 regular season away contests.

As far as Cincinnati are concerned, they netted 39 goals from the same amount of home matches, the third best attacking return in home fixtures in the Eastern Conference.

Despite finishing down in ninth place, 10 points came from their closing five fixtures, and Pat Noonan's team have now emerged victorious from five of their last seven games, albeit only losing to Nashville in this exact fixture on November 7.

Prior to the first leg, Cincinnati had the benefit of shaking off a lack of match sharpness through registering a 3-1 win at Orlando City SC in the Eastern Conference wildcard tie.

Former Manchester United left-back Tyrell Malacia is an option to replace Bryan Ramirez at left wing-back for FC Cincinnati.

Tom Barlow, with 10 goals and five assists during the regular season, may also be introduced into the final third, but Noonan is not expected to overly-tinker with a winning team.

Meanwhile, Jack Maher could be brought into the Nashville SC backline after Maxwell Woledzi suffered an injury during the second half of the last game.

Yazbek and Bryan Acosta are battling for one place in central midfield, while Warren Madrigal may be recalled on the flank.

Sam Surridge, who has netted 21 league goals this season, has scored five times in his last seven appearances.

Celentano; Doudera, Malatini, Robinson, Smith, Malacia; Martinez, Gidi; Nwobodo; Evander, Denkey

Schwake; Najar, Maher, Palacios, Baker-Whiting; Yazbek, Corcoran; Espinoza, Mukhtar, Saad; Surridge

Although FC Cincinnati will be confident of making home territory count, we can only anticipate an improved performance from the Eastern Conference regular season winners. With that in mind, Nashville could edge this fixture by the odd goal in three to take the tie to a decider.

#2112. Palmeiras vs Estudiantes - prediction, team news, lineups

By Adepoju Marvellous | 26 Nov 2026 11:46, Last updated: 26 Nov 2026 11:48

Palmeiras and Estudiantes meet on Saturday afternoon in the 2026 Copa Libertadores final.

The clash at the iconic Estadio Centenario revives memories of the 1968 final, when Estudiantes claimed the first of their four titles and became the first Argentine side to beat a Brazilian team in the final.

Palmeiras, meanwhile, are chasing a fourth continental title after reaching three of the last six finals.

After 58 years, Palmeiras have the chance to renew their rivalry with Estudiantes in a final worthy of the occasion. The 1968 final was decided over three matches: the Argentines won the first leg in La Plata before losing at the old Pacaembu.

In the decider at the same Estadio Centenario, Estudiantes won 2-0, with goals from Ribaudo and Juan Ramon Veron. One of the goals in Estudiantes’ first Libertadores triumph was scored by the father of the club’s current president, Juan Sebastian Veron.

Palmeiras have not been as dominant in 2026 as in previous years. They drew two of their six group-stage matches, but have taken the lead in eight of their last 10 continental fixtures during the knockout rounds.

In the round of 16, the Alviverde gained revenge on Cerro Porteno, completing a quartet of meetings between the sides this campaign.

Abel Ferreira's men then needed penalties to overcome LDU Quito in the quarter-finals and Fluminense in the semi-finals. Goalkeeper Carlos Miguel proved crucial on both occasions, making three saves in total.

After losing the national title to Flamengo last Saturday, Palmeiras fielded a reserve side in Tuesday’s visit to Chapecoense and suffered a 3-0 defeat.

Although the result did not change their position in the Brasileiro table, Palmeiras are now winless in four matches across all competitions. They have also conceded in each of their last five outings.

Like Palmeiras, Estudiantes have also faced the same opponent four times in this edition: Flamengo, the reigning continental champions and two-time Brazilian champions.

In Group A, Alexander Medina's men suffered a narrow defeat at the Maracana and drew 1-1 at the Jorge Luis Hirschi. In the semi-finals, the Pincha held the favourites to two goalless draws before goalkeeper Fernando Muslera starred in the shootout, saving penalties from Jorginho, Lucas Paqueta, and Bruno Henrique.

That trademark Argentine resilience was also evident against Universidad Catolica in Chile, and especially across the two quarter-final matches against Corinthians.

Estudiantes have conceded an average of just 0.5 goals per match across their last 10 Libertadores games. Since the round of 16, Muslera has been beaten only twice.

Their main concern is at the other end: Estudiantes have scored no more than once in nine of their last 10 matches in the tournament. A closely fought contest in Montevideo could therefore be in prospect.

Palmeiras’ medical staff are racing to get defender Murilo fit after he sustained a left-thigh injury. If he is unavailable, Bruno Fuchs is expected to join captain Gustavo Gomez and Alexander Barboza in the back three, ahead of youngster Luis Benedetti.

Forward Paulinho is also a major doubt after developing inflammation in the muscle and bone tissue in his calf. Even if he recovers, he could miss the Copa do Brasil final.

Uruguay international full-back Joaquin Piquerez was rested against Rubro-Negro but is expected to return on the left in his home country.

Fellow full-back Jefte remains provisionally suspended pending a hearing before the Anti-Doping Sports Justice Tribunal (TJD-AD).

For Estudiantes, former Sao Paulo midfielder Gabriel Neves is still recovering from a fractured arm and is likely to miss out.

Neves joins midfielder Baltasar Rodriguez, who is suspended after being sent off in the second leg of the semi-final.

Joaquin Correa sustained a grade-two muscle injury to his right thigh against Talleres on November 7.

With the usual recovery period around three weeks, the former Botafogo forward is undergoing intensive treatment in a bid to make the bench.

Miguel; Gomez, Murilo, Barboza; Giay, Freitas, Pereira, Piquerez; Arias, Roque, Lopez

Muslera; Mancuso, Nunez, Palacios, Rojo; Piovi, Burgos, Castro, Palacios, Cetre; Carrillo

With two solid, well-organised defences and goalkeepers in good form, a high-scoring contest seems unlikely.

Palmeiras may dominate possession, but Estudiantes could respond with a set-piece goal if they fall behind.

If the final goes to penalties, Muslera could have the chance to redeem himself after a difficult World Cup.

#2111. Raul Jimenez’s late penalty seals share of spoils for Wolves against Blackburn

An injury-time penalty from returning hero Raul Jimenez ensured Wolves came away with a point in their Championship opener after Blackburn had produced a comeback.

Wolves led through Marshall Munetsi’s cool finish on 18 minutes only for Sean McLoughlin’s first Blackburn goal to draw the visitors level on 27 minutes with a well-timed run and header.

The visitors turned the game around on 48 minutes through Andri Gudjohnsen’s close-range strike only for substitute Jimenez to level the game three minutes into injury time from the spot after Ryan Alebiosu handled the ball in the box. Jimenez and his wife marked his return to Molineux by giving 5,000 fans black scarves emblazoned with the words ‘Si Senor’ - a nod to his song - and Jimenez did not disappoint as he rifled in his penalty.

LATE DRAMA! 😱Raul Jimenez cooly converts from the penalty spot, deep into added time to save a point for Wolves 🔥 pic.twitter.com/5h2lMxcHNU

Cesar Peixoto wants Wolves to play with their heads rather than their hearts after the home side rescued a late point in the first game of the Championship season following relegation. Wolves replaced Rob Edwards with Portuguese coach Peixoto during the summer and he felt his side’s loss of organisation allowed Blackburn to take control of the game.

Peixoto said: “One point is not what we want, we want three points. We lost some organisation in the second half and played more with our hearts and not our heads and brains.

“We have to keep our mental stability during the 90 minutes because we’re better when we’re organised. We need to play our way with our identity. It’s a lesson for us to understand when we lose organisation it’s more difficult for us and more easy for the opposition.

“We’re working hard to bring more solutions [into the club] but we need to work hard with the players we have. It’s the first game, there’s a lot to work on and a lot to improve.”

Tony Mowbray took charge of Blackburn in the league for the first time since returning to the club for a second stint in the dugout. Rovers recorded their lowest league finish since Mowbray’s first stint in charge of the club last term but the Blackburn boss is confident his side have enough to beat the drop and climb up the table this campaign.

Mowbray said: “Ultimately, it feels like a defeat, it’s a bit disappointing and a bit frustrating. I just felt a lot of decisions were starting to swing their way. It’s harsh to take, I don’t think they deserved a second goal. We asked the players to be brave and we were collectively very strong.

“Everybody has us relegated, probably on the back of finishing 20th last season and losing a couple of our better players and not spending much money, which is a good motivation for the team. We deserved to win the game, we showed there’s enough quality to give teams problems and I hope there’s help on the way [in the transfer window].”

The first chance of the season came the way of Blackburn as a cross-field pass found Alebiosu down the right, who floated a ball into the box only for an unmarked Oladipo Afolayan to head wide from eight-yards out.

However, Wolves scored the first goal of the new campaign when Fer Lopez played a defence-splitting through ball into the path of Munetsi, who coolly found the net with a first time effort from the edge of the penalty area.

"A vibrant Molineux rejoices!" 🎉Marshall Munetsi cooly slots home for Wolves to give them the lead on the opening night of the Championship season 🥶 pic.twitter.com/tVHdbNv3Hq

Blackburn drew level through an unlikely source just nine minutes later as McLoughlin headed in his first-ever goal in English football in more than 150 games when he met Ryoya Morishita’s cross from the right at the near post.

The home side had chances to retake the lead before the break as Lopez curled a shot narrowly over the top from 20-yards while Kieran Trippier teed up Mateus Mane for a chance as the teenager found the top of the net from distance.

Blackburn turned the game on its head minutes after the break as Gudjohnsen turned home Afolayan’s deflected ball in from the right from close range to stun Molineux into silence.

"It's brilliantly worked by Blackburn Rovers!" 💥Andri Guðjohnsen finishes from close range to turn the game around and the visitors the lead 👏 pic.twitter.com/vkKQQezicK

Peixoto introduced Jimenez from the bench on the hour mark but even the Mexican could not lift Wolves with Blackburn comfortable for long periods as frustration grew in the stands.

However, Alebiosu handled Jackson Tchatchoua’s cross from the left in injury time and the referee awarded a spot kick which allowed Jimenez to mark his Wolves return with an equaliser from 12-yards as he sent Balazs Toth the wrong way.

“I think it is a really harsh one. He [the referee] has to be sure at such a critical time of the game. And I do not think he can be sure. I think he has almost had a guess that he is in the box. It is such a big call.”

“We see the ref’s position. The fact the ref makes the decision to give the penalty in my opinion, it is a bigger decision to give it then to not. We have got the luxury of the replays and we are still not sure. His whole body is outside the box.”

“It looks outside the box to me [the penalty incident]. The still picture suggests to me it was outside the box. We can get better and we will have to. We have to be more dominant with the ball at times and be better in attack. We have done a lot of work on that in pre-season. Having been in this league a long time, the power of the dressing room is crucial and any motivation tool you can you use is important. I think we will be fine. You have to enjoy football and love it. I think the boys enjoyed it tonight.”

“Obviously frustrated. The boys did everything to get the three points but it is gutting in the end. I thought I was outside the box when the ball came in and the decision went against us. They are saying it is in the box and it is frustrating. We dug deep and thought we had it. I cannot fault everyone as we all put in a shift, it was just the final details with the penalty decision. We deserved the three points. If we play like that every week, we will go far in the table.”

Raul Jimenez reacts after his late penalty saved a point for Wolves against Blackburn 🗣️ pic.twitter.com/aXvVeWw6fc

“Before the game we were predicting Wolves would be right up there [this season] and we thought it would be a comfortable win. But that was not the case.  Blackburn gave a really good account of themselves and had lots of openings. [Cesar] Peixoto will be breathing a massive sigh of relief with that handball.”

“Wolves got away with one if I am being really honest. In terms of their second-half performance, they did not do anywhere near enough. I thought Blackburn were excellent throughout and deservedly took the lead. It is a huge decision [to give the penalty], but in the end Raul Jimenez steps up to get his side the point. [Cesar Peixoto] will be delighted to get something in the end.”
