Bentley spills the corner but gets a free-kick. Moments later, the referee blows his whistle for the final time and the game ends level at 2-2. A dramatic opening night of the new Championship season at Molineux. If the Championship continues in this vein all season, it will be one hell of a year.

Blackburn nearly score a late winner as Garrett’s shot is tipped over the bar by Bentley for a later Blackburn corner.

He sends Toth the wrong way and calmly slots home to bring Wolves level in added time. That penalty decision will certainly create some debate. The Championship is always full of drama and we get some on opening night.

LATE DRAMA! 😱Raul Jimenez cooly converts from the penalty spot, deep into added time to save a point for Wolves 🔥 pic.twitter.com/5h2lMxcHNU

It just had to be. Jimenez rescues Wolves from the penalty spot.

Drama on the opening night of the campaign.

Relief for Wolves, heartbreak for Blackburn.

Late drama at Molineux! The cross is sent in from the left and the ball strikes the arm Alebiosu on the edge of the box. Was it inside or outside the penalty area? It is definitely handball but was it outside the box? It was very, very close but there is no VAR in the Championship so the penalty will stand.

Four added minutes. Are we on the verge of seeing the first shock of the new Championship season on opening night?

Four minutes added. Not even a reaction from the home crowd.

Wolves are desperately running out of time and Blackburn still look comfortable.

Home fans are beginning to filter out slowly.

Mosquera is still struggling but he has come back on whilst limping around. I cannot imagine there will be too much in terms of added time, either three or four minutes would be my guess, no more.

Ohashi is played in behind and goes down under a challenge from Toti on the edge of the Wolves box but those appeals are waved away.

Mosquera has gone down again and he may not be able to continue as he receives treatment on the sidelines. Wolves may be ending this game down to 10 men.

Fevrier’s shot from outside the Wolves penalty area is blocked and spins behind for a corner in front of the travelling Blackburn fans. The corner comes to nothing.

Wolves struggling to create anything of note even with Jimenez’s introduction.

Centre-back Mosquera has gone up front but appears to be struggling with an injury. He’s jogged back to his natural position now.

Desperate long balls being sent forward but Blackburn look comfortable for now. The home fans are quite audibly unhappy with this second-half showing.

Wolves do not currently look like scoring an equaliser.

Mosquera is struggling with a knock but Wolves made all five changes. If he is unable to continue, Wolves will have to finish off with ten men.

The frustration inside Molineux is growing and the home fans want to see some more urgency from their side as they trail with under 10 minutes left.

Blackburn are now going to make their final two alterations of the night as Ribeiro and Garrett come on.

Wolves are going to make their final two substitutions as Lima and Chirewa replace Moller Wolfe and Andre.

Blackburn are looking pretty comfortable right now defending their lead and counter-attacking effectively.

Wolves want a penalty as Armstrong goes down inside the box after making a run in behind but the appeals are waved away. Rightly so as Armstrong was not fouled.

Wolves are pushing to find an equaliser but are struggling to carve out many genuine opportunities.

Ohashi is penalised for a foul on F Lopez not far outside the Blackburn penalty area and that was pretty harsh on Ohashi. Jimenez is lining this one up as is F Lopez.

The former takes but his effort hits the wall.

Wolves’ frustration right now is summed up by a dreadful cross from F Lopez from the right side of the box that goes behind for a goal kick. The TV cameras then turn to some of the Wolves fans, who look rather forlorn in the stands.

Double changes for both sides. For Wolves, Doyle and Tchatchoua come on. For Blackburn, Fevrier and Ohasi enter the fray.

You’d have thought a goal had gone in such is the reaction from the home crowd to Jimenez’s introduction. I have to say, I’m surprised Mane has made way but Jimenez will play through the middle with Armstrong moved out to the left.

Blackburn made a change earlier in the half with Wolverhampton-born Sam Morsy on for his Rovers debut. One manager going for it as the other attempts to shore things up.

Wolves’ last shot on target was all the way back in the 18th minute, when Munetsi put them in front. That is in fact their only shot on target tonight, compared to Blackburn’s four.

Should Wolves have been given a penalty? Jimenez goes down after Atcheson did stick out his leg but was there enough in it to give a penalty?

Jimenez is indeed now going to come on and Molineux rises to welcome him onto the pitch. The Jimenez scarfs go up around the ground and his song is sung. Mane goes off.

Raúl Jiménez receives an incredible reception from the Wolves fans as he makes his league return for the club 👏🇲🇽 pic.twitter.com/mPE7pvTJZt

No messing about from new Wolves boss Cesar Peixoto with his side behind and discontent brewing in the stands, Raul Jimenez is going to be introduced. His name has been sung throughout the game with a hero’s welcome imminent.

Can he inspire a comeback? Wolves and Peixoto will certainly hope so.

Munetsi, the scorer of Wolves’ goal, has a shot deflected from the edge of the box and it goes behind for a corner. Gudjohnsen, the scorer of Blackburn’s second, heads away at the front post.

You can sense the frustration growing from the home fans inside Molineux, with a few groans creeping in.

Blackburn are the first to turn to their bench as one experienced midfielder replaces another with Morsy coming on for Forshaw.

Wow! What a start to the second half. Blackburn have turned it around, Tony Mowbray clearly inspired his troops at half time.

Molineux is silenced now with Blackburn fans making all the noise. Some home supporters were still taking their seats as Gudjohnsen turned the ball home.

Can Wolves turn the game back in their favour? It’s all a bit slow now from the hosts followed by the first signs of disgruntlement from the home supporters.

And to top it off, Mane is booked for simulation.

The first Wolves player into the book tonight is Mane and it is for simulation. Watching the replays, that feels like a fair call as Mane threw himself to the ground and it probably was a dive.

Molineux is stunned as the underdogs take the lead inside the opening five minutes of the second half. Tony Mowbray’s side have started well since the break and are rewarded. Afolayan is found making a run into the penalty area on the right side of the box and his cross is slotted home by Gudjohnsen. His father Eidur would have been happy with a striker’s finish like that.

"It's brilliantly worked by Blackburn Rovers!" 💥Andri Guðjohnsen finishes from close range to turn the game around and the visitors the lead 👏 pic.twitter.com/vkKQQezicK

Morishita drives forward through the Wolves half and towards the penalty area. He lays it off to Afolayan, whose effort is tipped over the bar by Bentley for a Blackburn corner, which is headed and then cleared away by Wolves.

We are back under way at Molineux. No changes for either side at the break.

Possession: Wolves 52%-48% BlackburnShots: 10-4Shots on target: 1-2Corners: 3-2Touches in opposition box: 9-7

An intriguing first half as the whistle sounds and both teams head down the tunnel.

Both teams scored when the other seemed on top slightly.

Wolves have probably shaded it with those efforts from Lopez and Mane which went narrowly over.

Plenty to think about for the two managers.

The first half is brought to a close at Molineux and an engaging opening 45 minutes of the new Championship season results in this game being level at 1-1 at the break. Munetsi put Wolves in front before McLoughlin equalised for Blackburn.

Forshaw is the third Blackburn player into the book tonight as the referee goes back to penalise the midfielder for an earlier foul on Andre. That was not Forshaw’s first foul either.

With no VAR or hydration breaks, there will be just one added minute at the end of this first half.

Mane wins it back for his side on the edge of his own penalty area and drives forward. He takes it across the halfway line before feeding a lovely pass to his right to find Armstrong, whose shot is blocked.

Mane comes close to a stunner. Trippier rolls the ball across the face of the penalty area to 18-year-old Mane, who takes on the shot first time but his effort goes just over the bar.

Wolves come close to restoring their lead. Mane drives inside from the left and plays the ball forward to F Lopez, who takes one touch before getting his shot away from just outside the box. He tries to pick out the top corner but it just misses the target.

Like London buses, two yellow cards come at once and another Blackburn player is into the book. It is their captain Cantwell who receives a yellow card for delaying the restart. Cantwell was not too impressed with that decision.

Both teams off the mark for the season now, a brilliant near-post run and header from McLoughlin who scores his first-ever goal in English football to level the game up.

Molineux briefly stunned into silence. A slight delay from the fans celebrating the header as well.

The first booking of the Championship season goes the way of Blackburn left-back Pickering. It is a pretty simple decision for the referee as Pickering cynically pulls back R Gomes, who had spun him, and rightly receives a yellow card.

R Gomes delivers a cross from the right side of the Blackburn box across the penalty area but Armstrong does not pre-empt that and is not on hand to meet it. However, Alebiosu, with Mane not far behind him, puts it behind for a corner. Trippier delivers and the Blackburn keeper makes a meal of it, missing his punch out but then scrambles back towards his own goal to gather the loose ball.

The visitors went behind against the run of play but probably deservedly they are back on level terms. Morishita clips in the cross from the right to the near post, where McLoughlin meets it first and guides a powerful header into the far corner. Two goals in the opening half an hour of the new Championship season.

"They STUN Molineux!" 😳Sean Mcloughlin delightfully heads past Dan Bentley to equalise for Blackburn 👏 pic.twitter.com/v5qLhJ0wBt

Blackburn are getting great joy down their right through Alebiosu and Morishita, with the latter winning them a corner as Toti is forced to head behind from a cross. Pickering sends it into a good area and the touch comes off the head of Munetsi, whose header loops into the air and goes just over his own bar for another corner on the other side.

Cantwell’s delivery to the far post is headed away.

We have the first goal of the season and it’s the returning Marshall Munetsi who gets it.

A cool finish from the midfielder after a bit of a drab start from the home side.

That will please the new Wolves boss. Such a simple move but the home side ripped Blackburn wide open with two passes.

Work to do for the visitors now who had probably started the better of the two teams before the opener.

Wolves fans make their feelings about VAR known following the goal. No VAR in the Championship of course but no VAR was needed for that anyway. A perfectly good goal.

Mane cuts inside from the left onto his preferred right foot and curls a dangerous ball into the box that is punched away by Toth in the Blackburn goal. 18-year-old Mane was one of very few bright spots for Wolves last season and in many ways it is a surprise that no Premier League teams have lured him back to the top flight quite yet. That could though still happen before the deadline.

The first goal of the Championship season comes against the run of play. It is a clinical, ruthless goal that comes from an incisive pass and clinical finish. The pass is fed through by F Lopez to pick out the run of Munetsi, who is cool, calm and collected as he finishes past Toth. Blackburn had been the better side up until that point but Wolves take the lead at Molineux.

"A vibrant Molineux rejoices!" 🎉Marshall Munetsi cooly slots home for Wolves to give them the lead on the opening night of the Championship season 🥶 pic.twitter.com/tVHdbNv3Hq

A shot on target for Blackburn but an easy save in the end. Alebiosu, who has looked good in these early stages, drives down Blackburn’s right before cutting it back to Morishita on the edge of the box. Morishita takes one touch before shooting but his effort is tame and straight at Bentley.

Blackburn have definitely been the better side in these early exchanges, dominating possession and looking a threat going forward.

Blackburn may be heavy underdogs tonight but they have settled down well away at one of the favourites to get promoted. Tony Mowbray may well be seeing this as a good time to play Wolves away.

Alebiosu shows quick feet down Blackburn’s right and clips a cross to the back post, where Afolayan meets it but his header is just off target.

Safe to say the doom and gloom of last season appears to have lifted at Molineux. The home fans in fine voice early on here.

By contrast, Kieran Trippier’s first touch of the season is greeted by loud boos by the travelling Blackburn supporters.

In fact, every Trippier touch is greeted by a chorus of boos. Will they keep that up all game?

They’ve certainly not forgotten his Burnley connection.

New managers and new signings galore. This should be a fascinating spectacle.

He may not be in the starting XI tonight but Jimenez is being serenaded by the home fans on his return to Wolves after a few years away at Fulham. Jimenez and his wife have paid for 5,000 scarves for the Wolves fans tonight.

Blackburn boss Tony Mowbray did say ahead of the game that Wolves have not been used to winning games over the last 12 months so can the visitors take advantage of that tonight? Blackburn are underdogs tonight and not too much is expected of them this season so surely they have nothing to lose this evening?

Due to his Burnley ties, new Wolves defender Trippier is booed by the travelling Blackburn fans. On the other side of defence for Wolves is Moller Wolfe, who started for Norway against England in the World Cup quarter-finals in Miami.

After Jeff Beck’s Hi Ho Silver Lining rings around the stadium and tributes paid to former Wolves goalkeeper Phil Parkes, we are under way at Molineux. The 2026/27 Championship season is officially under way.

Both sides emerge from the tunnel and we are just moments away from kick-off on opening night in the Championship.

When Wolverhampton Wanderers last attacked the Championship under a Portuguese manager new to English football, it proved a vintage season.

Molineux was under the spell of Nuno Espírito Santo in that 2017-18 campaign, and the big question is whether César Peixoto can lead a repeat nearly a decade later.

Peixoto was a surprise appointment in June, replacing Rob Edwards, and he has been entrusted with the mission of returning Wolves to the Premier League at the first time of asking.

“I have changed some things with set-pieces and some things in other moments of the game because I want the team to be organised and understand all the different moments within a match. During pre-season we have scored some goals from high pressing, but we will not be able to press high in every moment of every game. The players have to understand the game and recognise what each moment requires. It is important that we work as a team. When we can press high, we’ll press high. When we need to defend in a mid-block, we will stay organised. When the opposition pushes us deeper, which will sometimes happen, we have to be organised there as well.

“There are a lot of crosses in the Championship, for example, so the team needs to understand what to do in those situations. We want to play our football, be offensive and react strongly when we lose the ball, but we also need to understand the different moments of the game. I think we are at a good level to attack the Championship.”

Our experts have been having their say ahead of the new Championship season. A number of them believe Wolves will be promoted, including John Percy:

It is difficult to look beyond West Ham. Nuno Espírito Santo romped to the Championship title with Wolves in 2018 and remains a shrewd operator (along with his No 2 Rui Pedro Silva), while convincing Jarrod Bowen to stay is significant. I am going to go for Wolves and Middlesbrough as the other two promoted clubs.

The boys are in the Black Country 👋 #WOLvROV | #Rovers 🔵⚪️ pic.twitter.com/WjuGwrxYtQ

🔢 Team news...2⃣ changes✅ Kristi Montgomery & Andri Gudjohnsen come in🆕 Sam Morsy on the bench🤕 Augustus Kargbo, Dion De Neve, Scott Wharton, Moussa Baradji, Mathias Jorgensen & Lewis Miller on the injured list#WOLvROV | #Rovers 🔵⚪️ pic.twitter.com/esyE5Fibqc

The 2026/27 Championship season kicks off at Molineux as Wolves host Blackburn, with the home side hoping to put the bad memories of last season firmly behind them. There are new playoff rules in place for this season, with six places now up for grabs in the play-offs.

Wolves are aiming to return to the Premier League at the first attempt, having endured a miserable season in the top flight last year as they finished bottom with just three wins to their name. It has been a summer of change at the club as they chose to change managers. Rob Edwards lost his job in what was a shock move and was replaced by 46-year-old Cesar Peixoto, with this being his first head coaching position outside of his home country Portugal. Back in 2017/18, Wolves won the Championship title led by Peixoto’s compatriot Nuno Espirito Santo.

“I am ready,” Peixoto told Wolves’ club website. “We have worked very hard during pre-season, and I think we have had a great pre-season. All the players have worked very, very hard and they understand what I want from them. I am ready and confident going into the first game. Football is football, whether it is in Portugal or England, but there are different characteristics to the game here. We have watched, observed and analysed the Championship. We did that work before I came to the club, so we know what to expect.

“It is going to be a tough season with good teams, but we are prepared to fight for the three points game after game and week after week. We know what we want from the team and we know what our big goal is. We’re going to work very hard and fight to achieve that goal.”

They have brought in vast experience on the player side in the shape of Kieran Trippier and Raul Jimenez, who is back at Molineux three years after leaving for Fulham. Although they have lost Brazilian midfielder Joao Gomes to Aston Villa, most of their squad has stayed intact as they pursue promotion back to the top flight. It is a special year for the club as they celebrate their 150th anniversary.

Blackburn, led by experienced manager Tony Mowbray, did not enjoy a great season in the Championship last year, finishing down in 20th place and just five points above the relegation zone. 14 years have now passed since Blackburn last played in the top flight.

“I think it is a tough start,” Mowbray said. “On paper, I would suggest the first couple of games, particularly, jumped out as a tough start. One of the Premier League sides from last season, away from home. They have got a very talented squad, a pretty expensive squad. They have got some very good individual talent. Yet, there is an argument to say they have not won many football matches in the last calendar year. So, what we get, we will have to wait and see.”

These sides last met in the FA Cup during the 2024/25 season, with Wolves winning that fourth-round tie 2-0 thanks to goals from Matheus Cunha and Gomes, both of whom are now plying their trade elsewhere. The omens would appear to be on Wolves’ side as they are unbeaten in their last eight league meetings with Blackburn. Both sides progressed to the second round of the League Cup last weekend, with Wolves beating Port Vale 3-0 and Blackburn overcoming Burton Albion 2-1.

#2110. Milan, a gennaio il primo blitz per Endrick. Chance superiori se Vinicius resta al Real Madrid

Il Milan non molla la presa su Endrick e prepara l'assalto per portarlo già a gennaio o a luglio in rossonero. Nonostante le 5 vittorie nelle ultime 6 gare di campionato e il successo in Europa League ad Atene della squadra di Ruben Amorim, Gerry Cardinale considera il 20enne brasiliano la priorità assoluta per potenziare l'attacco. Attualmente fermo per un infortunio muscolare, l'attaccante del Real Madrid soffre uno spazio ridottissimo sotto José Mourinho: appena 160 minuti disputati finora con il Real Madrid contro i 248 giocati nelle sei gare con la Seleçao tra settembre e novembre. In un reparto affollato da Mbappé, Vinicius, Diomande, Arda Güler, Brahim Diaz, Espí e Bellingham - con Rodrygo pronto a rientrare a inizio 2027 - i suoi agenti chiederanno presto chiarezza a Florentino Pérez. Lo riferisce La Gazzetta dello Sport.

La partita di Valdebebas passa anche dal futuro di Vinicius Junior, rappresentato dalla stessa agenzia Roc Nation Sports. Nonostante il rinnovo fino al 2032 da oltre 20 milioni netti a stagione, l'avvio da 2 soli gol, l'ombra ingombrante di Mbappé e i fischi del Bernabeu hanno alimentato i sondaggi di Al-Ahli, Arsenal e PSG - a caccia di rinforzi dopo gli stop di Kvaratskhelia e Doué - ma Vini non intende lasciare Madrid, lasciando così spiragli concreti alla partenza di Endrick.

Per superare il veto di Mourinho e battere la Roma, il Milan fa leva sul rapporto privilegiato tra RedBird e Roc Nation, rafforzato da Brett Yormark - ex dirigente dell'agenzia e fratello di Michael Yormark - alla guida della neonata Milan Sports & Entertainment. Pronta e da presentare sul tavolo un'offerta da 60 milioni più bonus con clausola di recompra (in stile Nico Paz-Como), un ruolo centrale nel 3-4-2-1 di Amorim e l'assist decisivo di Kakà.

#2109. Champions League, le possibilità degli azzurri: cosa serve al Napoli per i Playoff

Il pari di Manchester ha dato qualche certezza in più al Napoli e ora gli azzurri sono più vicini all’obiettivo Playoff, priorità più volte indicata dalla società e dal tecnico Massimiliano Allegri. Il Napoli ha 4 punti in classifica e con 3 gare piuttosto alla portata la sensazione è che la qualificazione ai Playoff sia ora più vicina.

Sette punti in tre partite per avere la certezza, ora non serve solo vincere in tutte le gare ma gli azzurri hanno un jolly, da giocarsi probabilmente nella sfida contro il club belga del Club Brugge.

Nelle prossime tre sfide il Napoli di Massimiliano Allegri affronterà Club Brugge e Viking in casa e gli azeri del Sabah in trasferta, tutte squadre contro il quale il Napoli parte sulla carta favorito. Gli azzurri devono arrivare almeno a 10 punti, ma possono chiudere qualsiasi discorso a quota 11 punti e quindi 7 punti almeno in tre partite.

Un pari casalingo contro il Brugge e due vittorie contro Viking e Sabah chiuderebbero il discorso e gli azzurri si garantirebbero i Playoff con la possibilità di andare agli Ottavi di finale.

#2108. West Ham United vs Stoke City - prediction, team news, lineups

By Carter White | 26 Nov 2026 11:46

Aiming to move themselves back into the top half of the Championship, struggling West Ham United welcome promotion-chasing Stoke City to the London Stadium on Saturday afternoon.

The Hammers' woeful run of form continued at the Fratton Park base of Portsmouth earlier in the week, whilst the Potters picked up yet another Staffordshire victory, this time at the expense of Millwall.

Back in the Championship for the first time since 2012, West Ham United were expected by many to sit at the top of the rankings and waltz to a Premier League return, however, the East Londoners hit a new low on Tuesday night.

The Hammers extended their winless run to a worrying six matches (D2 L4) at Fratton Park, where a first-half effort from Odin Bailey was enough for Portsmouth victory, with the visitors once again failing to break down a stubborn second-tier defence.

Since the early-November arrival of Scott Parker to the managerial hotseat, West Ham have collected just two points across a quartet of fixtures, dropping down to 13th spot in the Championship table - a staggering 11 points behind first-placed Middlesbrough.

Following three away matches in their past four, the Hammers are back at their London Stadium base on Saturday, when Parker's men will be looking to halt a five-game winless run (D2 L3) at the East London venue.

In fact, West Ham rank 17th in terms of home form so far this season, with the pre-season title favourites last picking up three points in front of their own supporters on September 11, when Wrexham were hit for six without reply.

After placing between 16th and 18th in each of the past four Championship seasons, Stoke City have swapped midtable mediocrity for a Premier League chase in 2026-27, winning 11 of their past 14 league fixtures.

The in-form Potters have now gone seven games (W6 D1) without defeat after Wednesday night's impressive showing against Millwall, with George Hirst grabbing the headlines once again courtesy of a game-winning brace.

After recording five consecutive league victories for the first time since the days of Tony Pulis in February 2008, Stoke have moved up to second spot in the Championship table, level on points with leaders Middlesbrough.

The Staffordshire side managed just six league wins on the road last season but could equal that tally already with success this weekend at the London Stadium, where the Potters last played in April 2018.

That East London clash represents the most recent meeting between West Ham and Stoke, with a 90th-minute leveller from towering striker Andy Carroll forcing a share of the spoils following Peter Crouch's opener.

Looking for his first victory as West Ham boss at the fifth attempt, head coach Parker could make changes from the side that was defeated by Portsmouth.

However, there is little room for manoeuvre at right-back, where Joel Veltman should start in the absence of Kyle Walker-Peters (muscle).

Young goalkeeper Finlay Herrick could play once again, with Danish stopper Mads Hermansen dropped to the bench for the past two games.

Stoke suffered a major injury blow during the win over Birmingham City last weekend, with Djibril Soumare picking up a muscular injury.

In the absence of the influential midfielder, Jack McGlynn could start in a deeper role alongside fellow summer arrival Ethan Galbraith.

West Ham United possible starting lineup:

Herrick; Veltman, Mavropanos, Kilman, Mayers; Soucek; Bowen, Engels, Mukasa, Solomon; Piroe

Johansson; Wilmot, Lawal, Graham; Johnson, McGlynn, Galbraith, Devenny; Manhoef, Bae, Hirst

Without a victory under new boss Parker, West Ham's ambitions of an instant Premier League return are fading quickly.

Stoke will be in the mood to take full advantage of a Hammers side devoid of confidence, however, we feel that a share of the points is forthcoming at the London Stadium.

#2107. Birmingham City set sights on La Liga attacker with talks underway

Birmingham City are being linked with the La Liga forward ahead of the January transfer window. BCFC have the chance to freshen up their squad with some reinforcements this winter as they eye promotion from the Championship under the guidance of head coach Chris Davies.

Romero, 26, is under contract at Sevilla until June 2028 meaning he still has just under two years left to run down on his deal in Spain. He has made 11 appearances in all competitions this season and has chipped in with three goals and two assists.

In this latest update regarding his situation this winter, Ficherio claim he has emerged on the radar of the Blues as they eye a potentially ambitious swoop for his signature. The report suggests that talks have already begun with his representatives over a possible switch over to England.

Birmingham City are in the play-offs after 17 fixtures along with Wolves, West Brom, Southampton, QPR and Swansea City and they won 2-0 away at Burnley last time out. They are back in action this weekend at home against Bolton Wanderers.

Birmingham City’s Luis Vazquez is facing an uncertain future. BCFC only signed him four months ago but he has struggled to make an impact and is reportedly wanted by Racing Club.

Marvin Ducksch could also leave in January. Therefore, the Blues will need to bring in some competition for August Priske up top.

Romero was said to be on Sheffield United and Ipswich Town’s shortlist back in July last year. However, nothing materialised in the end and he stayed put at Sevilla.

Birmingham City could see the Spaniard as someone to bolster their attacking department. As well as down the middle, he can also play on the left flank as well if needed.

Romero has enjoyed plenty of game time at Sevilla in the Spanish top flight over recent years and would be an eye-catching addition for Birmingham City if they managed to get him through the entrance door. He would inject more quality into BCFC’s options going forward.

The Andalusia-born man rocked up at Estadio Ramon Sanchez-Pizjuan back in 2019. He then broke into the first-team 2023 and has since been a regular.

Davies’ men will be full of confidence after their victory at Burnley. They will be eager for more of the same against Bolton Wanderers as they look to close the gap on Middlesbrough, Stoke City and Wolves.

#2106. Donyell Malen, 2026’s top scorer, on the radar of Europe’s biggest clubs?

By Axel Clody | 26 Nov 2026 12:02

Donyell Malen has been a new man since arriving at Roma. With the Dutch striker the top league scorer in Europe's big five leagues in 2026, can he join one of Europe's best clubs?

Struggling at Aston Villa, Malen joined Roma in January 2026 on a £1.7m (€2m) loan. The Dutch striker has stepped up a level since moving to the Italian capital, and he is enjoying an exceptional 2026 calendar year with 26 league goals.

That makes him the top scorer across Europe's five biggest leagues over that period, ahead of Harry Kane and Kylian Mbappe (21 goals). His current success has not gone unnoticed and led Roma to invest £21.3m (€25m) to sign the best recruit of 2026 permanently.

Malen has not been affected by the permanent transfer and is continuing on excellent foundations at the start of the season. He now has 12 goals in 11 Serie A matches, and must also add five goals and two assists in five Champions League matches.

The versatile forward, who was often used on the flanks at Borussia Dortmund and Aston Villa, has been installed as the number nine by Gian Piero Gasperini with great success.

The Dutchman's performances are logically starting to attract more prestigious suitors.

With Kane injured at Bayern Munich and Doue and Kvaratskhelia injured at PSG, Malen's name has been cited as a target for both giants.

The two-time European champions are looking for a versatile forward for January, which is exactly what Malen is. His profile fits Paris's needs.

However, PSG do not want to overpay, and as things stand Roma have no interest in selling their star man, who is under contract until June 2030, in the middle of the season unless an exceptional offer arrives. A transfer in January therefore looks very unlikely, even to Bayern Munich, where he would also make a lot of sense with Kane absent for several months.

On the other hand, if Malen continues in this vein, the approaches should multiply next summer. A move to one of Europe's heavyweights could also appeal to the Roma forward, with the Italian club potentially struggling to keep hold of him and to turn down huge offers.

#2105. Newcastle kit fiasco sums up haphazard pre-season

Newcastle’s pre-season friendly against Bayer Leverkusen at St James’ Park took a bizarre turn when a kit clash prompted the hosts to change shirts midway through the first half.

The German team’s white away kit with blue trim proved hard to distinguish from Newcastle’s home strip, which has a white back and blue stripes across the shoulders.

During the first-half hydration break, Newcastle decided to change into their lilac third kit, which improved the viewing experience for fans who took to social media to question the original choice of kits.

Newcastle would go on to lose the game 2-1 in new manager Matthias Jaissle’s first game at St James’, a result which followed a 3-1 reverse against Everton on Wednesday evening.

Leverkusen, the 2023-24 Bundesliga champions now managed by Spaniard Carles Martínez Novell, scored after just 44 seconds through a powerful Ibrahim Maza strike.

Jaissle’s team found their feet as the first 45 minutes progressed, with Yoane Wissa their biggest attacking threat. The striker endured a difficult first season at the club, but his performances at the World Cup with DR Congo appear to have restored confidence.

Newcastle were level just before half-time, when Malick Thiaw rose highest to plant home a back-post header from a corner. Former Brentford goalkeeper Mark Flekken appealed in vain for a foul, but there was no infringement.

After changing all 10 outfield players at half-time against Everton, Jaissle was more circumspect with second-half substitutes; Rory Finneran for Lewis Miley was the only change before the 80th minute.

Newcastle faded though, and the game was won by Victor Boniface scoring just a few minutes after coming on as a substitute. The Nigeria striker wrong-footed Sean Steur with an impudent piece of skill before rolling the ball into the bottom corner.

The North-East club are coming to terms with the departures of Anthony Gordon, Sandro Tonali and Bruno Guimarães this summer, as well as manager Eddie Howe. They have one more pre-season friendly to play, against Strasbourg on Sunday, before they begin their Premier League campaign at home to Liverpool.

Newcastle United (4-2-3-1) Hornicek; M Miley (Pivas 81), Thiaw, Botman, Hall; Steur, L Miley (Finneran h-t); Elanga, Woltemade (Neave 86), Barnes; Wissa. Subs Charlton, Harris (g), Sanusi, Johnson.

Bayer Leverkusen (4-2-3-1) Flekken; Vazquez (Tape h-t), Bade, Tapsoba, Gutierrez (Belocian 70); Garcia, Fernandez (Terrier 70); Maza (Boniface 80), Tillman (Doganay 70), Moreira (Tella 80); Kofane (Schick h-t). Subs Hofmann, Arthur, Oermann, Poku, Blaswich (g), Alfa-Ruprecht, Hawighorst, Lomb (g).

Referee Michael Oliver (Durham). Attendance 39,059

A second pre-season defeat in a week for Newcastle. Just like against Everton, they faded in the second 45 minutes but Jaissle decided to leave some tired legs out there for fitness purposes. The performances of Barnes and Wissa were promising, but Newcaslte are giving up chances.

Newcastle had a free-kick with the last kick of the game, but Harvey Barnes loops the delivery straight into Flekken’s gloves.

Schik with a chance to wrap up the victory, but Hornicek saves before Thiaw clears to deny Terrier a tap in on the rebounds.

There will be just three minutes of added time to play.

Elanga fouled near the touchline, but time is running out. The free-kick won by Neave came to nothing.

Chance for Barnes! Good combination play involving Elanga and Wissa down the right, and the ball flashed across the six-yard box. Barnes almost steered it in, but did not make firm enough contact.

Newcastle attacking with more urgency since conceding, but there are moans and groans in the ground at a misplaced Elanga pass. Sean Neave has just replaced Woltemade, and the 19-year-old wins a free-kick in a crossing position with his first action of the game.

The striker has only been on the pitch a few minutes, but Boniface has restored Leverkusen’s lead. Lovely chop inside the box, which sent Steur for a hot dog, before passing the ball into the far corner. No celebration of note from a player who could be on the move this summer.

Newcastle making just their second change of the game: Pivas replaces Mason Miley at right-back.

Leverkusen have made two more changes: Moreira and Maza off, Tella and Boniface on.

The corner is straight down the throat of Flekken, and that prompsts some groans from the home fans.

Newcastle having to defend deep for long periods, but Elanga did well to win a free-kick with little support. Moreira dragged the winger down. Then Wissa’s cross is turned behind for a corner.

Woltemade’s control lets him down as he tries to bring the ball down on halfway. The game is being played at a snail’s pace at the moment, but Leverkusen are dominating the ball.

Play has now resumed. Garcia does well to defend after Lewis Hall won the ball back in a dangerous position.

Time for another hydration break and Leverkusen are making three more substitutions. We await further changes from Jaissle. Should the game finish level, there will be a penalty shoot-out to decide the winner of the Visit Malta Cup.

Rather like the mid-week friendly against Everton, Newcastle have faded after half-time. Struggling to get into the game in any meaningful way. Slick football from Leverkusen but Fernandez fails to bring the ball under his spell in the box when there was time and space to shoot.

Leverkusen just inches away from regaining the lead. It was an incisive run off the left from Afonso Moreira, who tried to place the ball into the far corner but his attempt rolled just wide of the post.

Good defensive header by Lewis Hall before Wissa wins a foul on halfway. Newcastle feeding off scraps at the moment. Elanga goes too early and is flagged offside.

Bayer Leverkusen have had the better of it in this half, but some of the shooting has been wayward. This time is it Schik’s turn to slash a shot over the bar. Fouls committed by Finneran and Botman.

Newcastle with a three-on-three on the break after Wissa does well to win the ball back on halfway, but Woltemade was too slow to get his shot away at the end of the move. More good play from Wissa.

Half a chance for Fernandez to shoot from the edge of the D, but he was leaning back and ballooned the shot miles over the bar.

Finneran, the youngster in Newcastle’s midfield, is caught on the ball but then does well to recover it. I reckon there will be a raft of changes on the hour mark.

Corner for Leverkusen after Moreira gets forward and attacks Mason Miley. The German side have additional aerial threat with Schik on the pitch. Woltemade does well to head clear.

Bayer Leverkuen kick off in the second half.

Jaissle made 10 against Everton, so wants his starters to build minutes today. Rory Finneran is coming on for Newcastle, an 18-year-old academy player. He replaces Lewis Miley in midfield. Czech striker Patrik Schik is on for Bayer Leverkusen.

The scoreline is a fair reflection of an even first half, with Wissa Newcastle’s best player. The story though, is that kit clash that prompted Newcastle to change into their lilac third kit halfway through the half.

Just the two minutes of stoppage time to play.

Newcastle equalise from a set-piece. Flekken is claiming a foul, but it is a legitimate goal. The goalkeeper was nowhere to be seen on a corner that looped all the way to be back post, and he seemed to lose his bearings allowing Thiaw to find the far corner.

Newcastle have a corner after another move in which Wissa was lively. His performances have been a ray of light in pre-season, he really struggled in his first season at the club.

Lewis Hall takes the free-kick, but he scoops it well over the bar. Elanga delivers a low cross from the right, but Garcia is well positioned to clear.

Newcastle enjoying a lot more possession now, but you cannot say they look like equalising.

That was better though, more zip in the attack with Elanga laying the ball off for Wissa and Fernandez is booked from dragging the Newcastle forward to the ground. Free-kick in a shooting position.

Good play from Lewis Hall, skipping beyond two challenges to escape from a tight spot but then his crossfield pass is intercepted. Moreira passes the ball straight out of play for a Newcastle goal kick, when there was a chance for Leverkusen to punish the hosts on the break.

Elanga tries to take on Gutierrez but his low cross is too close to Flekken. Gutierrez then buys a foul when under pressure from Lewis Miley. Game has become quite bitty following those stoppages. Maza then chops down Harvery Barnes.

The game is back under way. Changing kits has improved things visually but it is still not ideal. You wonder how on earth two clubs of this stature failed to liaise on this before the game.

Bayer Leverkusen are still on top, but play has been stopped for a hydration break. Some boos from the stands, it is not an especially hot day.

On the subject of the kit clash, Newcastle are now changing into their lilac away kit. Shades of Manchester United at the Dell all those years ago.

A short stoppage in play after Tapsoba receives a knock in the small of the back from Newcastle’s goalkeeper Hornicek. Both managers get their players over for a tactical conflab.

Leverkusen look very accomplished in possession, they are moving the ball well through the lines. Kofane was a big threat when they played Arsenal in last season’s Champions League and they are looking to find him with crosses. Mason Miley defends well at right-back, but at the expense of a corner.

Flekken called into action again, Newcastle starting to warm to their task. It was direct play from first Elanga and then Barnes, but his cross deflects towards the goalkeeper and he parries away. Then Barnes is fouled, and Newcastle have a free-kick in a crossing position near the left corner of the penalty area. Poor delivery from Barnes, Flekken claims.

Newcastle fans sing “walking in a Keegan wonderland” in unison. Bade is then penalised for catching Woltemade late as they contested an aerial challenge.

Newcastle’s best moment so far: Wissa peels off the shoulder of Bade and tested Flekken with a volley from a tight angle. The goalkeeper saved and Leverkusen scramble clear, but it was good movement from the Newcastle striker.

Leverkusen continue to make the running, enjoying more possession and most of it is coming inside Newcastle’s half. Goalkeeper Flekken takes a beat inside his own six-yard box, and his pass out is almost cut out by Woltemade.

Newcastle’s players are wearing black armbands in memory of Kevin Keegan.

There is a slightly awkward clash of kits on display: both shirts feature a lot of white, and the blue trim around the shoulders of Newcastle’s home kit is quite similar to Leverkusen’s away strip.

Kofane, who impressed up front last season in Germany, throws himself at a cross.

Fans were barely settled in their seats when Maza’s vicious left-footed strike from outside of the area flew past Hornicek. Cracking goal, not much the goalkeeper could do about it.

Matthias Jaissle walks to the home dugout for the first time as manager. Just eight days until the proper stuff begins against Liverpool, a fixture which tends to throw up entertaining games. Bayer Leverkusen keep the home players waiting in the tunnel.

The former Liverpool defender has been linked with a move to Arsenal this week. Quansah is with his team-mates but not part of the matchday squad.

Bayer Leverkusen XI: Flekken; Gutierrez, Bade, Fernandez, Moreira, Tillman, Tapsoba, Arthur, Garcia, Maza, Kofane

Subs: Blaswich, Lomb, Hofmann, Terrier, Schick, Oermann, Tape, Poku, Vazquez, Boniface, Tella, Alfa-Ruprecht, Doganay, Hawighorst

Subs: Harris, Charlton, Pivas, Sanusi, Johnson, Finneran, Neave

Today's team to take on Bayer Leverkusen in the @VisitMalta Weekender 👊 pic.twitter.com/ZfRvucBXSq

Newcastle United continue their preparations for the new season with a home friendly against Bayer Leverkusen, hoping to improve on their pretty limp showing against Everton at Murrayfield.

New manager ​Matthias Jaissle is ‌refusing to dwell on the departure of key players that has left his club in turmoil going into the Premier League season, insisting that he sees opportunity in the situation. Jaissle was recruited from Saudi Pro League side Al-Ahli after Eddie Howe left last month, with the German coach joining a club who had lost Anthony Gordon, Sandro Tonali and captain Bruno Guimaraes in quick succession.

“It’s a challenge, a big challenge, always when there’s a transitional period. ‌But it’s ‌not time to ⁠complain, it doesn’t make sense to see the negative things,” Jaissle said.

“We want to see the chance in that situation and that’s our approach from day one. We are here to do our ⁠best job. We want to support the players, the current squad.

“There will be adjustments to see in the coming days, weeks. But for now it’s only focused on the daily work, the work we can influence. That’s all that ​matters now.”

The 38-year-old coach arrived in Newcastle ‌having won two Austrian Bundesliga titles with Red Bull Salzburg before leading Al-Ahli to back-to-back AFC Champions League crowns.

He remained tight-lipped about new signings.

“Obviously, in that transition phase, the players who left the club were ‌great ​players, great characters, experienced ⁠players. This is now a vacuum,” he said.

“Either we adjust something in the squad, by signing these players, these profiles, or other players ⁠need to step up and fill ​these positions, these leadership roles. I’m quite optimistic, we can work in both scenarios.”

Jaissle said he had a ​clear vision of how he wants his team to play.

“The phrase how Newcastle ‌should play like is front-footed, intense, ​aggressive and of course also successful. ​In the end, it’s all about what ⁠I can ⁠say and it’s also why I’m sitting here,” he said.

“The approach we have as a coaching team and the identity of Newcastle is famed for, it’s so linked so close together - which is a perfect fit in my eyes and ​that’s going to be exciting.”

#2104. Bromley vs Burton Albion - prediction, team news, lineups

By Calum Burrowes | 26 Nov 2026 12:11, Last updated: 26 Nov 2026 12:20

Meeting for the first time in their history, League One newcomers Bromley will be looking to climb out of the relegation zone when they welcome Burton Albion to Hayes Lane on Saturday afternoon.

The Ravens will be aiming to make it three league matches unbeaten after following up a win over Reading with a home draw against Wycombe Wanderers on Tuesday night, while the Brewers will be hoping to end their three-game losing streak after suffering a 2-1 defeat to an in-form Luton Town side last Saturday.

After securing promotion to the third tier for the first time in their history last May under the stewardship of long-serving manager Andy Woodman, Bromley were expected to face a difficult first season at this level, with the club having every reason to stick by the man who had guided them to two promotions in three seasons and taken them from the National League to League One.

However, Woodman's five-and-a-half-year spell at the club came to an abrupt end in late October after a 4-0 defeat to Leicester City left the Ravens 23rd in the table following 10 matches, a decision that came as a surprise and disappointment to many Bromley supporters.

The London-based side turned to former MK Dons, Charlton Athletic, Oxford United and Salford City boss Karl Robinson, who will take charge of his fifth match as Bromley manager this weekend.

Robinson's arrival was followed by exits from both the FA Cup and EFL Trophy in his opening two matches, but he has since managed to steady the ship in the league, guiding his side to a narrow victory over Reading before they followed that up with a draw at home to Wycombe Wanderers during the week, leaving him unbeaten in League One since taking charge.

Those four points from their last two outings have provided some much-needed momentum ahead of a busy festive period, although Bromley remain in the bottom four with 13 points to their name, meaning a fourth league victory of the season could be enough to lift the Ravens out of the relegation zone ahead of the final month of the calendar year.

As for Burton Albion, they make the journey to Greater London from Staffordshire hoping to bring an end to a three-match losing streak in League One that has seen them slip out of the top half and move dangerously close to the relegation places.

A victory in their final home fixture of October against Leyton Orient was followed by defeats to Wycombe Wanderers, MK Dons and, most recently, Luton Town, with the second of those losses proving particularly damaging as they were beaten 6-2 at Stadium MK.

The Halloween defeat to the Chairboys was at least followed by a 4-0 FA Cup victory over AFC Rushden and Diamonds at the start of November, but that remains Burton's only win in their last five matches across all competitions, a run that has also included a defeat to Notts County in the EFL Trophy.

Although their heavy loss to MK Dons came against an in-form side, Burton's own defensive record has taken a significant hit, with Gary Bowyer's men now having conceded 29 goals in their 16 League One outings, a tally bettered only by their upcoming opponents.

With that said, only two teams in the bottom half of the table have scored more than the Brewers, highlighting that Bowyer's side have an attacking foundation to build on if they can turn their fortunes around sooner rather than later.

A victory in their first-ever meeting with Bromley could lift Burton as high as 15th and give them a seven-point cushion over the League One relegation zone.

Having collected four points from his opening two league matches in charge of Bromley, Robinson will be keen to maintain some consistency ahead of the weekend fixture, and he has the option of keeping the same side after coming through Tuesday's draw without any further injury concerns.

Defender Kamarl Grant scored their only goal last time out and could partner makeshift centre-back George Saville once again, unless former West Ham United and Augsburg defender Reece Oxford is ready to make his second appearance of the season after featuring against Reading in what was his first competitive outing in more than four years.

Robinson's relationship with Ravens winger Jodi Jones is reportedly strained following events during their time at previous clubs, and the former Notts County attacker is expected to remain on the bench for this encounter.

As for Burton, they are likely to be forced into a change after Joe Lewis was forced off with an injury late in their defeat to Luton, with the defender expected to miss out and potentially be replaced by Jason Sraha.

Lewis is set to join several teammates in the treatment room, with Finn Delap, George Evans, Kyran Lofthouse and Caylan Vickers also expected to remain sidelined due to various injury and fitness concerns, leaving the Brewers unable to fill their matchday squad in recent weeks.

Matthew Dennis, however, returned to the team after missing just one fixture and could come back into the starting lineup to lead the line, potentially at the expense of either Mark O'Mahony or Gbemi Arubi.

Emeka Adeile has started just two league matches all season but has scored in consecutive appearances and is expected to retain his place out wide on Saturday afternoon.

George; Ifill, Grant, Oxford, Mendy; Pinnock, McKiernan, Conteh, Archer; Whitely, Adeboyejo

Addai; Sibbick, Sraha, Tilt; Rodrigues, Chauke, Krubally, Adeile; Collar; O'Mahony, Dennis

With both sides heading into December embroiled in an unwanted battle near the bottom of the table, they will be eager to pick up valuable points, move further clear of danger and build some much-needed momentum.

We expect the pair to play out a 1-1 draw, allowing Robinson to maintain his unbeaten start to life as Bromley manager in League One.

#2103. De Ketelaere guida l’Atalanta verso Lecce: al Via del Mare obiettivo continuità

L’Atalanta e il suo trascinatore Charles De Ketelaere. Il campione belga vuole continuare la sua stagione positiva tra gol e assist, anche se ora conta soprattutto risollevare fin da subito la squadra in piena zona Europa: pur sapendo che occorrono principalmente umiltà e tanto lavoro. Poi la gara contro il Lecce nella quale sarà necessario dare ancora più continuità, anche perché CDK con i giallorossi ha sempre fatto la differenza nelle annate precedenti.

Charles De Ketelaere contro il Lecce, nelle 5 partite giocate con la maglia nerazzurra dalla stagione 2023/2024 fino a oggi, ha siglato 3 gol e 2 assist, incidendo praticamente in tutti i match contro la squadra pugliese. Con il Borac giovedì sera ha offerto una prestazione sottotono, ma ciò non toglie la grandezza sul campo del trequartista belga, importantissimo per un’Atalanta che ora deve ritrovare continuità.

Contro il Lecce la sua titolarità sarà pura formalità nella trequarti, dove al suo fianco avrà con grande probabilità sia Jonathan Rowe sia Elmas, dimostrando grande intesa e concretezza. Ora la parola passa al campo, dove De Ketelaere non farà mancare impegno e una grande impronta sulla partita allo stadio Via del Mare di Lecce lunedì sera.

#2102. Update emerges on future of Manchester City’s goalkeepers – it could impact QPR and Pierce Charles

QPR signed Pierce Charles on loan from Manchester City in the summer, following his switch from Sheffield Wednesday to the Premier League giants. The 20-year-old was viewed as a big talent before this season, but his starring performances for the Hoops have put the wider footballing world on notice.

As Charles impresses at Loftus Road, change could await in the goalkeeping department back at his parent club. The Athletic tips Manchester City to make changes in between the sticks by moving out both Gianluigi Donnarumma and backup Geronimo Rulli in 2027.

Rulli, 34, is only cover, but Donnarumma has drawn criticism for his shortcomings in possession, given that City need a goalkeeper who is comfortable in helping in build-up phases. This is undoubtedly Charles’ standout attribute, and it may well see him accelerated into first-team contention at the Etihad Stadium.

To make it clear, there is nothing suggested about a Charles recall from QPR in The Athletic’s new report. In October, it was confirmed that Manchester City have an option to bring him back in January if they wish, but there were no plans at the time.

Regular EFL viewers have known about Charles’ potential for some time. He emerged on the first-team stage at Sheffield Wednesday and quickly impressed with his distribution, showing composure beyond his years and a passing range that wouldn’t look out of place in midfield.

Those same attributes in possession have been on show with the Hoops, but Charles is growing in confidence in commanding his box and claiming crosses too. A save percentage of 67.8% shows there is still room for improvement with regard to shot-stopping (FotMob).

However, Charles’ passing abilities make him an ideal fit for Manchester City’s style, and he seems bound to get a chance in the future. Accuracy is one thing, but the Northern Irishman’s technique sees means he can play flat balls into the attackers too, allowing them to bring it down on their chest or with their feet rather than challenging for headers with big Championship centre-backs.

This allows QPR to get the ball forward quickly and keep it there. It is a rare ability in goalkeepers, and one that will not go unnoticed at Manchester City.

Manchester City need a goalkeeper comfortable in build-up, and Charles is just that. However, at this moment in time, it would be a big risk to pull him from his QPR loan and throw him into the starting lineup.

As touched on before, his save percentage shows he still needs to improve as a shot-stopper. The progress he is making in West London suggests he can grow and become a starting option for City further down the line, but at present, the areas he still has to improve could be exposed at an elite level.

It means the Hoops should be confident of keeping Charles for the season – it would be best for him to stay for the full campaign too.

However, in the interest of staying prepared, it might be wise for the R’s to eye potential replacements. That way, if Manchester City do make a bold move to recall Charles, they are ready to act swiftly when January comes around.

#2101. Lincoln City vs Wolverhampton Wanderers - prediction, team news, lineups

By Darren Plant | 26 Nov 2026 12:15

Wolverhampton Wanderers make the trip to Lincoln City on Saturday afternoon looking to strengthen their hold of a top-three position in the Championship table.

Meanwhile, Lincoln play host to one of the form teams in the division having just sacked co-managers Chris Cohen and Tom Shaw.

Positives can be taken from Lincoln - last season's League One champions - reaching this stage of the season sitting three points above the relegation zone.

Nevertheless, Tuesday's 4-0 defeat at Charlton Athletic represented their fifth successive defeat and was the final straw for Lincoln's hierarchy.

Despite Cohen and Shaw arguably performing better than anticipated in their surprise role, Lincoln had also only recorded four points from their last 10 games, losing on eight occasions.

A change in direction has, theoretically, been made at the right time, and a permanent successor is expected to be selected ahead of the trip to Cardiff City on December 5.

For now, first-team coach Shaun Pearson and fellow backroom staff members will attempt to guide Lincoln to just their second home win in nine matches, their only such success having come against struggling Burnley by a 4-1 scoreline last month.

As for Wolves, they make the trip to the ProAmpac Stadium having recorded a 3-0 win over Derby County and having had the most shots (39) in a Championship fixture in 11 years.

For all the criticism that has come Cesar Peixoto's way, Wolves remain in third spot with 31 goals and 34 points from their 17 games.

An eight-match unbeaten streak has been put together, the West Midlands outfit picking up six wins and two draws since October 16.

Lincoln may take belief from Wolves only prevailing from four of their eight away games. That said, Peixoto's side have already made trips to the likes of West Ham United, Birmingham City, Sheffield United, Middlesbrough and Wrexham this season, suggesting that it is a far better record than in black and white.

Wolverhampton Wanderers Championship form:

Pearson is expected to make a number of changes to the Lincoln XI, while he may also change formation for a game against possession-heavy opponents.

Sonny Bradley, Yunus Konak, Dom Jeffries and Freddie Draper are all in contention for recalls to the team.

However, midfielder Conor McGrandles is again expected to miss out through injury.

Meanwhile, Peixoto could make a number of alterations, with Toti Gomes and Marshall Munetsi among those who could feature for Wolves.

Rodrigo Gomes may also get the nod over Rafiki Said in the final third, while Adam Armstrong scoring on five of his last six appearances will see him keep his place ahead of Sean Neave.

Lincoln's change in management team may lead to a positive response. Nevertheless, they are playing a Wolves side that are quietly going about their business, and we feel that the visitors' quality will shine through.

Peterborough United will be looking to pick up an important victory when they welcome rivals Cambridge United to the Weston Homes Stadium on Saturday.

The hosts currently sit 22nd in the League One table, while the visitors are 11th.

Peterborough head into this local derby after ending a four-match winless run last weekend when they beat promotion chasers Plymouth Argyle 2-0.

Harry Ashfield opened the scoring in the first half, before Harry Leonard converted a penalty in stoppage-time to secure all three points.

The win was a welcome boost for Robbie Savage's side, who are fighting for survival and remain in the relegation zone with 13 points from 14 games.

However, they will be looking to carry the momentum of last weekend's win into Saturday's clash, with the Posh targeting back-to-back league victories for the first time since February.

It is also worth noting that Peterborough have a strong recent record against their rivals, having won their last five meetings across all competitions.

Cambridge, meanwhile, are without a win in their last five league games and suffered a disappointing 3-0 home defeat to league leaders Stockport County last Saturday.

Ollie Norwood broke the deadlock early in the second half with a stunning free kick, before Ethan Ennis doubled their lead and Gassan Ahadme scored an unfortunate own goal.

The defeat leaves the U's 11th with 21 points from 15 matches, having suffered just one defeat in their first 10 games before going five without a win.

Head coach Neil Harris has admitted his side have not been "tough to play against" in recent weeks and is keen for them to "get back to being that team".

Cambridge may be forced to make at least one change, with the availability of Kelland Watts in doubt after he was forced off against Stockport.

#2099. Rashford returns and Fernandes misses another penalty as Man Utd humbled by Amorim

Bruno Fernandes missed a penalty for the second successive pre-season friendly as Manchester United were beaten 4-2 by former head coach Ruben Amorim’s AC Milan.

Fernandes, who rolled a spot-kick wide in a shoot-out against Leeds in midweek, was gifted the chance to open his pre-season account in Wroclaw, Poland.

His effort was on target this time, but Milan keeper Lorenzo Torriani dived to his right to beat the shot away.

Fernandes did at least get an assist, taking the second-minute corner which was powerfully headed home by Harry Maguire to give United the lead.

Amorim’s Milan equalised after a defensive mix-up allowed Gonçalo Ramos to break into the area and square for Samuel Chukwueze to roll home.

United were awarded their penalty just before half-time after Koni De Winter tugged back Youri Tielemans, but Torriani guessed correctly to leave Fernandes frustrated.

United retook the lead after Patrick Dorgu pounced on a loose backpass from Filippo Terracciano, but Milan equalised again when Chukwueze’s wayward shot was poked in by Alphadjo Cissè at the far post.

Marcus Rashford, who was frozen out by Amorim and loaned out to Aston Villa and then Barcelona, came on as a substitute on the hour for his first United appearance since returning from Spain.

But Milan went ahead in the 68th minute when Chukwueze’s cross was headed home by Ramos, and three minutes later former Chelsea midfielder Ruben Loftus-Cheek raced through to hit the fourth and cap a performance that underlines United’s need for reinforcements before their season starts in earnest next Saturday, away at Hull City.

Manchester United (4-2-3-1) Lammens; Mazraoui (Dalot 61), Maguire, Heaven (Martinez 61), Shaw; Tielemans, Santos (Mainoo 61); Diallo (Lacey 61), Fernandes, Dorgu (Rashford 61); Cunha (Mbeumo 61). Subs Zirkzee, Yoro, J Fletcher, T Fletcher, Amass, Collyer, Mee (g), Heath (g), Gabriel. Booked Dorgu.

AC Milan (3-4-2-1) Torriani; Terracciano (Gila 60), Winter (Vladimirov 84), Pavlovic (Diawara 84); Chukwueze, Musah (Ricci 76), Jashari (Modric 60), Estupinan (Bartesaghi 61); Loftus-Cheek (Comotto 84), Cisse (Saelemaekers 60); Ramos (Camarda 75). Subs Pittarella (g), Bouyer (g). Booked Estupinan.

Goals Chukwueze (37), Cisse (57), Ramos (68), Loftus-Cheek (71).

United’s pre-season ends in defeat. A wretched second half proves their undoing as Amorim’s Milan score four. Worth reminding that United led this match, twice.

Milan are playing keep-ball in these latter stages and although Mainoo regains possession, his attempt to play forward is cut out and Modric’s outside-of-the-foot pass finds Comotto, who heads straight at Lammens.

Four minutes of added time to be played...

United now raining crosses down on Milan’s box but Torriani equal to it every time.

Martinez has added a little bit of urgency to United’s build-up. Mbeumo tries to feed Shaw down the line but the ball is cut out.

More substitutions... Diawara, Vladimirov and Comotto enter the field of play in place of Pavlovic, De Winter and Loftus-Cheek.

Milan are now sitting off, settling into a back five as they look to preserve their advantage. Fernandes, Tielemans and Dalot combine well but such has been United’s fortunes this afternoon, the ball ricochets off the referee and play is halted.

Good work from Rashford in the corner to regain possession, but his ball into the box is not picked up by a team-mate.

More changes... Ramos and Musah make way for Camarda and Ricci.

Manchester United 2 AC Milan 4 (Loftus-Cheek) No sooner had the players returned after the second-half hydration break then Milan exploit United’s exposed left side (again). Ramos sends Loftus-Cheek through and the midfielder composes himself before bending the ball around Lammens.

Manchester United 2 AC Milan 3 (Ramos) That’s too easy. United fail to learn their lesson, Shaw not getting tight enough to Chukwueze and the winger’s cross is met by new signing Ramos, who powers a header back across goal and past Lammens.

Loftus-Cheek and Ramos are doing a good job of cutting off the pass into United’s midfield in their build-up phase.

Rashford (who makes his first United appearance in almost two years), Lacey, Dalot, Mbeumo, Mainoo and Martinez replace Dorgu, Amad, Mazraoui, Cunha, Santos and Heaven.

Manchester United 2 AC Milan 2 (Cisse) United have set a higher defensive line so far this half, with their front line pressing with more intensity to avoid the ball over the top. There are calls for offside, but with no VAR here, the goal stands.

Milan win a free-kick some 25 yards out after Fernandes clips Estupinan. Teenager Cisse steps up and fires straight into the United wall. Was always going to be difficult to trouble Lammens from there.

Manchester United 2 AC Milan 1 (Dorgu) United wisely decide to keep hold of the ball for a while, the midfield three rotating to disrupt Milan’s out-of-possession shape and open up passing lanes. A Milan throw-in from the right-back berth is precedes an awful backpass by Terracciano which is pounced on by Dorgu who curls the ball beyond Torriani.

United have looked bright in the opening moments of this half. They win a free-kick midway into Milan’s half. The ball is delivered to the back post and, after some 18-yard-box pinball, a relieved Torriano pounces on it.

Both teams remain unchanged as the second 45 minutes begin.

All square at the break. Tough to know what to make of that half – United looked solid enough and could have taken a lead into the interval had Fernandes converted his penalty, but they failed to create a wealth of clear-cut chances. It will be interesting to see how Carrick approaches this second half.

Milan have enjoyed just a tick over two-thirds of possession, which United have contributed to in part through some lackadaisical passing.

Fernandes misses a spot-kick for the second time in two friendlies! Here, Torriani guesses correctly, diving to his right to palm Fernandes’ strike away.

De Winter brings down Tielemans inside the box after Terracciano loses the ball attempting to play out from the back.

Manchester United 1 AC Milan 1 (Chukwueze) It’s all a bit messy. Jashari causes Maguire and Santos to crash into each other (Kaka at Old Trafford, anyone?) off a throw-in, Ramos taking over with the United pair out of commission. He finds Chukwueze who finishes the move off at the far post.

A Mexican wave has already begun. We’re only 21 minutes in...

Milan are indeed in Amorim’s preferred 3-4-2-1 formation. They circulate the ball in front of United’s first line of pressure in an attempt to calm things down.

Manchester United 1 AC Milan 0 (Maguire) Fernandes delivers an inswinger from the left and Maguire is there to meet it at the back post, striding past Musa to plant a header past Torriani.

United make a fast start, Dorgu flashing a cross-cum-shot across the face of goal. Torriani gets a fingertip to it and thte ball goes behind for a corner.

Manchester United defeated Leeds United on penalties to hoist the mighty Boyle Sports Cup at Croke Park. The match, a 1-1 draw, was brought to an abrupt end after five young pitch invaders ran to the middle of the field to take selfies with Bruno Fernandes. In the shoot-out it was James Justin who missed the “decisive” spot-kick, paving the way for Noussair Mazraoui to win it for United.

Marcus Rashford is on the bench this afternoon and could make his first United appearance since his cameo in a Europa League win over Viktoria Plzen in December 2024. He was frozen out under Amorim and has since been loaned to Aston Villa and Barcelona, but his Old Trafford future under Carrick remains unclear.

Michael Carrick believes Manchester United can push for the Premier League title, but has underlined the urgent need for signings.

The United head coach has taken heart from his squad’s promising preparations for the new campaign and, speaking at their Co Kildare summer base, he explained why he was confident they could build on last season’s third-place finish.

Carrick has been thrilled to hear United’s players discussing what they might achieve and has dared supporters to dream that they can get closer to last year’s champions Arsenal. But he is also a realist and recognises he is not blessed with options.

So far United have added Youri Tielemans, Andrey Santos and Karl Darlow, a reserve goalkeeper, but their £83.3m spend is significantly less than what newly promoted clubs Ipswich Town (£109.4m) and Coventry City (£92.2m) have invested in their squads.

Amorim’s side have gone intercontinental this summer, travelling from Scotland to Indonesia, via Australia, before arriving in Poland for the last of their four pre-season games.

At his AC Milan unveiling, Amorim was asked about the learnings from his United tenure that he would take into his new role. He said: “The first thing is it’s hard to explain the mistakes because for that I would have to explain the context of the last adventure.

“It’s hard to say to you every mistake. The only thing I can say is that I learned a lot and I made some mistakes.

“I didn’t have the opportunity, and I’m sorry for that, I didn’t have the opportunity to say something to the Manchester United fans at the moment and I’m really proud to be their coach for a year in that time.

“But now it’s a different history. There are a lot of things that I could do better in the last experience but sometimes it’s like that, you need to learn to find the right spot to reach a different level and that’s my thinking here.

“Everyone learns from their experience. I learned a lot, I did some mistakes I will try to change, there are some thing you will never change but I think I will be a better manager, that’s all.”

Welcome to live coverage of Manchester United’s final pre-season match, against AC Milan in Wroclaw, Poland. Michael Carrick’s side will look to apply the finishing touches to a promising summer on the pitch, which has included victories over Rosenborg and Atlético Madrid, plus a draw against European champions Paris St-Germain. The team will fly back to Manchester with the big ones, the ones they have always wanted – the Snapdragon Cup and the Boyle Sports Cup – in tow.

At the Tarczynski Stadium there will be a reunion with former manager Ruben Amorim, who was appointed AC Milan’s head coach in June, five months after being sacked by United. Following a frustrating season under Massimiliano Allegri in which Milan fell out of the Champions League spots on the final day, the Rossoneri said Amorim would bring a “modern dominant tactical approach”. His methods failed to cut through at Old Trafford, though, during a tumultuous 14-month reign which finally ended after the Portuguese “blew up” during a heated meeting with director of football Jason Wilcox. At Milan, Amorim has persisted with the 3-4-2-1 formation that was doomed at United.

Carrick made clear this week that United must strengthen if they are to challenge for titles. So far the club have brought in Youri Tielemans, Andrey Santos and Karl Darlow, but they are still in the market for a left-back and another midfielder. The future of Marcus Rashford has also been a topic of conversation among United fans. The forward has not played for his boyhood club since December 2024 but has joined up with his team-mates in Poland and it remains to be seen if he features in Carrick’s forward planning.

Kobbie Mainoo, who did not play a minute at this summer’s World Cup, and Lisandro Martínez have also joined up with the team this week and could be handed their first minutes of pre-season having been named in the matchday squad. For Milan, new signing Gonçalo Ramos – bought from PSG for £63m – will make his first appearance in the famous red and black.

#2098. Ds Sassuolo: "Napoli forte ma vogliamo rialzarci. Il club a gennaio non vende i big"

Il Sassuolo ha voglia di ripartire. La 13^ giornata di Serie A porta il Napoli al Mapei Stadium, con i neroverdi di Roberto D'Aversa che hanno voglia di dare conferme ai segnali incoraggianti visti col Genoa. In esclusiva per TuttoMercatoWeb, il direttore sportivo Francesco Palmieri ha scelto di presentare la sfida di domenica pomeriggio soffermandosi anche sulle insistenti voci di mercato che vedono proprio il Napoli su Sebastiano Esposito per gennaio.

"Sappiamo che sarà una partita difficilissima, il Napoli è una squadra molto molto forte. Sappiamo anche di essere in un momento complicato e in una posizione di classifica non buona, ma le squadre vanno affrontate tutte e noi siamo pronti per questa sfida", il primo pensiero del ds neroverde.

Il suo ragionamento prosegue, ancora sul Napoli: "Conosciamo il valore dell'avversario, ma noi vogliamo ricreare quelle belle cose che si sono viste nelle prime 6-7 partite di questo campionato. Dopo quelle c'è stato un momento di smarrimento, ma già nell'ultima sfida contro il Genoa ho visto segnali molto incoraggianti".

Nelle ultime ore avete perso per infortunio Adzic… "Dispiace, con Adzic e Leysen abbiamo perso due ottimi giocatori che non ci saranno per un po'. Ma abbiamo una rosa corposa e di qualità e l'allenatore saprà scegliere i giocatori giusti".

Inevitabile chiederle del mercato. A proposito di Napoli, le voci su Sebastiano Esposito si fanno sentire... "Il fatto che una squadra come il Napoli osservi i nostri giocatori fa piacere e ci inorgoglisce. Noi abbiamo puntato su Sebastiano in estate per questo ed il fatto che il suo nome sia accostato al Napoli e che sia arrivato in Nazionale dà ancora più valore alla nostra scelta. Noi adesso però dobbiamo pensare solo al nostro momento e a risollevarci in classifica".

Nessun pensiero quindi al mercato di gennaio? "In questi giorni ho letto, forse anche troppo, di queste attenzioni ma non abbiamo ricevuto chiamate. Sappiamo di avere giocatori importanti come Sebastiano, ma la nostra proprietà è forte e ha un progetto importante in mente. Storicamente a metà stagione non abbiamo mai venduto i pezzi pregiati e anche in questo caso non faremo partire nessuno".
